- **Achievement notifications (`.20`).** `sr_notify_milestone_achievement` adds the achiever's capital to a per-milestone *list* on every receiver (`sr_notify_achievers_<m>`) and queues one `.20`; each popup takes exactly one entry (`sr_take_milestone_notice_base`, an `else_if` chain) — the world first's entry before any other, since the list has no reliable order — and reads "world first" from the achiever's own `sr_was_first_<m>`. Two completions inside the 14-day delay — different milestones, or the same milestone by two countries — give two popups, each naming its own achiever, and option b's relations go to that achiever.
- **Programme loss (`.76`).** A lowered space-programme production method, or a lost / demolished building, fails every milestone its tier was carrying, and the journal entries notice on their own 4-day update schedule. Each `on_fail` calls `sr_report_program_loss = { MILESTONE = <m> }`: it records the milestone and only the first queues `.76`, five days out, so one change gives one report whose option tooltip lists the closed programmes. The text is read at fire time: building still there → a refit (a choice, the original "Program Discontinued" text); building gone → "The Silent Gantry", an involuntary loss.
- **Solar colonization "running".** Its journal entry stays open, frozen, while `pm_solar_colonization` is switched off (and after the last colony). `sr_solar_colonization_running` (active + production method on + not completed; also the gate of its four controls) is what events `.35/.44/.48/.49/.50/.53/.55` and `sr_boost_active_milestones` read, so no event narrates or moves a frozen programme. `.35` ("keep the settlements supplied") also needs a real colony (`sr_has_space_colony`).
- **Foreign powers in the text are real.** `.26` ("When Politics Reaches Orbit") names a space power our relations with have really soured (`sr_is_estranged_space_power`: rivalry either way, or relations at cold or worse) and is only in the safe pool while one exists. `.52` (joint orbital station) needs another UN member that has reached orbit (`sr_is_station_partner`), names it, and is worded as our own space agency's proposal rather than a claim about what that country did.
- **Rivals' estimated progress (display only).** The panel's Rivals rows show each rival's progress as an estimate inside a band: a yearly random offset per rival and milestone (`sr_fuzz_<m>`), a band of ±25% of the goal that the viewer's covert network narrows (±10% / ±5% at intelligence tier 1 / 2). Filled monthly for players only by `sr_rivals_estimate_refresh`; the design is in `docs/systems/journal_entry_systems.md` → Space Race → Milestone Panels, item 3.
- **Moon Landing Site:** Special event (space_race_events.5) offers Shackleton Crater (high risk, science windfall) vs Equatorial Plain (low risk, modest rewards).
- **Interstellar Probe:** Launches a probe to Alpha Centauri. On completion, spawns a passive `je_space_race_interstellar_results` JE that ticks 1/month for 132 months (~11 years) using a separate `sr_interstellar_transit_progress` variable (not shared `sr_milestone_progress`). When complete, fires event 60 (Interstellar Probe Data Received) which randomly selects one of 30 possible discoveries across 4 categories:
  - **Category I: Dead Worlds & Data** (40% chance, 8 results) — Barren worlds, asteroid maps, stellar remnants. Grants `sr_probe_dead_worlds_data` (modest prestige/research/innovation).
  - **Category II: Astrophysical Wonders** (30%, 7 results) — Ringed terrestrials, global oceans, runaway greenhouses. Grants `sr_probe_astro_wonders_data` (good prestige/research/innovation/cultural pull).
  - **Category III: Biological Discovery** (20%, 9 results) — Atmospheric biosignatures, alien vegetation, exotic biochemistry. Grants `sr_probe_biological_data` (major prestige/research/innovation/cultural pull).
  - **Category IV: Intelligence & Tech-signatures** (10%, 6 results) — Orbital debris, technogenic gases, artificial light. Grants `sr_probe_intelligence_data` (exceptional prestige/research/innovation/cultural pull).
  - Discovery selection uses two-stage `random_list`: first picks category by weight, then picks specific result uniformly within category. Each result event (601–630) records itself globally on first discovery (`sr_record_probe_result`: `sr_probe_found_<id>` = the discoverer). A later nation drawing the same result gets opening/closing lines that confirm the first finder's discovery, named, instead of "unprecedented data"; the rewards are the same (none is a first-discovery reward). Result events use triggered_desc blocks for the confirmation variant and the result-specific description.
- **Solar System Colonization (Repeatable):** Each completion establishes one colony at a random unclaimed location in the current stage. Colonies are tracked via global variables (`sr_colony_*`), making them first-come-first-served across all nations. Stage advances when all locations in a stage are claimed. The goal per colony rises with the stage (`sr_solar_colonization_goal`: 400 / 450 / 500 / 550 / 650). Once all 34 colonies across 5 stages are claimed, the entry's monthly pulse sets `sr_completed_solar_colonization` (and grants `sr_solar_system_trade`) for **every** colony holder within a month, whether or not its programme is running. Until 2026-10-03 that completion sat inside the bar-fill check, so only the country that claimed the last site finished at once; every other holder kept paying funding and approach for a bar that could found nothing until it next filled, years later, and a holder with the production method off never finished.
  - **Stage 1:** Mars (5) + Asteroids (5) = 10 colonies
  - **Stage 2:** Jupiter system (6) + Venus clouds (1) = 7 colonies
  - **Stage 3:** Mercury (1) + Saturn moons (5) = 6 colonies
  - **Stage 4:** Uranus moons (4) + Neptune moons (2) = 6 colonies
  - **Stage 5:** Kuiper Belt/Oort Cloud (5) = 5 colonies
- **Late-Game Economic Rewards:** Interstellar Probe grants one of 4 category-specific modifiers (see Interstellar Probe above), all colonies complete grants `sr_solar_system_trade`.
- **Commercial Space Industry (after the race, 2026-10-03).** Once a country has nothing left to spend progress on, `sr_space_race_concluded` (all seven single-goal milestones complete, `sr_all_colonies_complete`, `sr_has_running_milestone` false), the flat progress its sources still give is paid as Extraplanetary Base throughput: `sr_commercial_space_dividend` (`building_space_mine_throughput_add = 0.05`, a unit value) at `multiplier = sr_space_dividend_points`, re-applied by `sr_refresh_space_dividend` from the monthly country pulse (its one refresh site). The points are `modifier:country_space_race_progress_add` capped at `sr_space_dividend_max_points` (10, so +50%). Three choices pinned by `test_space_race_dividend.py`: the flat sum, not `sr_progress` (its 0.5 floor would pay every finished country) nor the multiplier (Antimatter Engines alone reach +250%); funding is out without special handling, since `sr_funding_progress` sits only on a billed entry; and the cap, because the Space Elevator alone gives up to 20 points against 9.25 for all 33 progress-granting colony specialisations together. At 5% a point a progress specialisation (0.28 on average) is worth about a quarter of a mining one (+6% throughput on average), so mining stays the long-term pick. A country that reaches the end after the last site is gone qualifies once its own milestones are done. The first time it applies, a player gets `space_race_events.77` ("The Commercial Space Age"), recorded in `sr_space_dividend_announced`. A revolution's winner gets the modifier back from the next monthly pulse: the test reads only inherited variables.
- **Milestone goals** (`space_race_values.txt`): Suborbital 50, Orbital 100, Moon Landing 200, Probe 150, Moon Base 300, Mars Landing 450, Interstellar Probe 600; colonization 400–650 per colony (above).
- **Progress Sources:** each month every running entry adds `sr_progress` = `modifier:country_space_race_progress_add` × (1 + `modifier:country_space_race_progress_mult`), at least 0.5. Everything reaches it through those two modifiers, so the tooltip breakdown lists every source (the overview's hover on the headline's "+N/mo" is that breakdown, `je_space_race_widget_pace_tt`):
  - `country_space_race_progress_add`: the Space Programme PM (Earth Orbit +0.5, every later tier +1, workforce-scaled), funding (`sr_funding_progress` on each running entry, +0.5 per funding level, stacking across entries, plus +25% space-programme throughput per level), the Space Elevator (+1 per level), the SpaceX, Lockheed Martin and Roscosmos company prosperity modifiers (+0.3 each), UN space partnership (`un_space_partnership_modifier`, +0.1), the `reusable_rocketry` and `space_colonization` techs (+0.2 each), and several choice-event and reward modifiers (probe target +0.3 to +0.5, `sr_mars_resource_extraction` +0.5, …).
  - `country_space_race_progress_mult`: the Ambitious approach (+50% per entry), the temporary safety review (−10%), the UN outer-space regime (leader −5%, laggard +10%), covert space espionage (+10%), antimatter engines (+5% each, workforce-scaled) and `principle_advanced_research_5` (+10%).
  - There is no Aerospace Industry or Space Mine term: the space mine only gates event `.53`, and `building_space_program` is a single-level monument. After the race the flow runs the other way: the leftover `country_space_race_progress_add` pays Extraplanetary Base throughput (Commercial Space Industry, above).
- **A revolution's winner continues the programme** (`docs/audits/civil_war_inheritance_audit.md` F5, #464). The winner inherits the loser's variables but none of its modifiers, and every entry it inherits active runs `immediate` again. So:
  - **`immediate` only creates what is missing.** Progress and funding are guarded with `has_variable`, so a milestone at 90 % stays at 90 %. That is safe for the native progress bar because an inherited record keeps the loser's bar: start date, baseline and goal are copied, not evaluated again (the German-revolution saves' `je_global_warming`: baseline 0.1 and goal 4.0 on the winner with the anomaly at 1.18). For a same-record re-activation inside a month (before the monthly cleanup clears the progress), `goal_add_value` subtracts the progress so the goal stays at `sr_<m>_goal`. Solar colonization's bar restarts at every colony, so it keeps progress only while it holds a colony (it cannot deactivate then); a colony-less programme still restarts, and a finished one is not re-opened. `je_space_race_interstellar_results` keeps its transit months the same way.
  - **Choice events are asked once per milestone** (`sr_<m>_choice_made`, checked in `immediate` and in the event's `trigger`). Before, a re-activation or an inherited entry asked again and could leave two answers and two modifiers.
  - **Every reward is rebuilt from a variable.** Milestone rewards (`sr_first_<m>` / `sr_<m>`) from `sr_completed_<m>` / `sr_was_first_<m>`, and the probe result (`sr_probe_*_data`, recorded as `<modifier>_held` by `sr_grant_probe_data`), come back in `sr_repair_after_civil_war`, which the shared hook `te_civil_war_on_won` runs for the winner. It is stateless: it adds a reward only where the record is held and the modifier missing, reads none of the shared layer's side variables, and removes nothing. Entry modifiers come back from each entry's `immediate` (`sr_sync_<m>_entry`) and, as a backstop, the monthly country pulse (`sr_sync_space_race_entries`, only for a country that has run a milestone: `sr_space_race_participant`): the approach modifier from `sr_safe_<m>` / `sr_ambitious_<m>` (and a stale one with no approach selected is removed), the choice modifier from the choice variable, the 68 colony specialisations from `<modifier>_held` (`sr_grant_colony_modifier`), and `sr_solar_system_trade` from `sr_completed_solar_colonization`. The monthly pulse also records a probe result or colony modifier granted before the record existed, so old saves migrate themselves. A loyalist winner still holds every modifier, so all of it is a no-op there. Timed modifiers (the completion events' themed rewards, failure penalties, the safety review) are not restored.
  - **The rebels start no programme of their own mid-war.** Suborbital flight's `is_shown_when_inactive` requires `is_revolutionary = no` (every later entry already needs a completion the rebels lack). Their own run would otherwise beat the loser's progress in the variable merge.

### Cross-System Connections
| System | Connection |
|--------|-----------|
| **UN** | `un_space_partnership_modifier` adds +0.1 progress (and prestige, science-ministry impact). The outer-space regime shares progress: `un_regime_space_leader_modifier` −5%, `un_regime_space_laggard_modifier` +10%. ISS cooperation event (52) fires for UN members with a station partner. UN resolution topic `un_topic_space` (event un_events.19). |
| **Private Companies** | `company_spacex`, `company_lockheed_martin` and `company_roscosmos` each add +0.3 progress through their prosperity modifier. Event 50 (private sector breakthrough) fires when SpaceX exists. |
| **Buildings** | `building_space_program` carries the programme tier (progress and the programme bools). `building_space_elevator` adds +1 progress per level. `building_space_mine` (extraplanetary base) adds no progress; it gates event 53, and after the race it receives the Commercial Space Industry throughput. Antimatter engines add +5% progress each; the Nanofabrication Center lowers setback risk (−1% per level). Events 41, 53, 55 create direct building interactions. |
| **Tourism** | Event 51 fires after orbital achievement if tourism industry exists. Grants `sr_space_tourism_boost` (tourism output +5%). Space tourism PM (`pm_space_tourism`) already exists in tourism industry building. |
| **Society Tech Events** | `society_technology_events.18` (space colonization) and `.19` (colonial governance) fire based on aerospace industry levels and space elevator. These are separate from but thematically connected to space race milestones. |

### Variables (Country Scope)
| Variable | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| `sr_active_*` | Active milestone flag (suborbital, orbital, etc.), set on activation and cleared on completion or failure. Test "any milestone under way" with the trigger `sr_has_running_milestone`, which reads these. A shared `sr_active_milestone` flag used to do that job; it was retired because the completion of any one milestone cleared it while others ran (#480) |
| `sr_completed_*` | Completed milestone flag |
| `sr_was_first_*` | Country was first to achieve milestone |
| `sr_progress_<m>` | Per-JE progress toward milestone (e.g. `sr_progress_suborbital`) |
| `sr_funding_<m>` | Per-JE funding level (0 to sr_max_funding_level) |
| `sr_safe_<m>` / `sr_ambitious_<m>` | Per-JE approach flags |
| `sr_failed_<m>` | Per-JE "setback not yet answered" flag (set in the pulse, cleared by the failure event's option). Which milestone an event acts on comes from `scope:sr_setback_<m>`, not from this flag |
| `sr_interstellar_transit_progress` | Interstellar probe transit progress (passive JE, not per-JE) |
| `sr_<m>_last_status` | Widget display state, 0-4. Single derivation site: `sr_set_milestone_status_base`. Absent when the milestone is not running. |
| `sr_<m>_setbacks` | Lifetime setback count for that milestone, shown by the widget. Incremented in `sr_count_setback_base`. |
| `sr_failure_cooldown` | Global months until failure can occur again (decremented once/month via on_action) |
| `sr_progress_boost` | **Proxy variable** — set before calling `sr_boost_active_milestones` (every running milestone) or `sr_boost_setback_milestone` (the failed one) |
| `sr_notify_achievers_<m>` | **Variable list** on each receiver: capitals of achievers whose `.20` popup is still pending, one entry per completion |
| `sr_program_lost_<m>` / `sr_program_loss_report_queued` | A milestone closed by a lost/lowered launch complex, waiting for the single `.76` report |
| `sr_moon_site_shackleton/equatorial/tranquility/far_side` | Moon landing site choice |
| `sr_orbital_*`, `sr_probe_target_*`, `sr_moon_base_*`, `sr_mars_direct/orbital_first/robotic` | The other milestones' choice-event answers; each drives an `sr_choice_*` modifier on the running entry, rebuilt from it by `sr_sync_<m>_entry` |
| `<modifier>_held` | The reward modifier `<modifier>` was granted: `sr_probe_*_data_held` (country), `sr_colony_*_held` (solar colonization entry). Rebuilt from after a revolution |
| `sr_colony_count` | Total colonies established by this country |
| `sr_space_dividend_announced` | `space_race_events.77` has been queued (or, for an AI, would have been): the Commercial Space Industry notice is given once |
| `sr_probe_launched` | Interstellar probe has been launched (triggers results JE) |

### Global Variables
| Variable | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| `sr_global_first_suborbital` | First suborbital claimed |
| `sr_global_first_orbital` | First orbital claimed |
| `sr_global_first_moon_landing` | First Moon landing claimed |
| `sr_global_first_probe` | First outer system probe claimed |
| `sr_global_first_moon_base` | First Moon base claimed |
| `sr_global_first_mars_landing` | First Mars landing claimed |
| `sr_global_first_interstellar_probe` | First interstellar probe launched |
| `sr_colonization_stage` | Current colony stage (1-5), advances when all locations in stage are claimed |
| `sr_all_colonies_complete` | All 34 colonies have been claimed |
| `sr_probe_found_<601–630>` | The country whose interstellar probe first returned that result |
| `sr_colony_*` (34 variables) | Individual colony claimed flags (e.g. `sr_colony_valles_marineris`, `sr_colony_europa`, `sr_colony_sedna`) |

### Important Notes
- **`building_space_program` is a monument** (`expandable = no`, single level). DO NOT check `level >= 3` etc. on it — use `building_aerospace_industry` for level-scaled bonuses instead.
- **Colony modifiers are JE-scoped.** All 68 colony modifiers and `sr_solar_system_trade` are applied to `je:je_space_race_solar_colonization`, not to the country directly. The colonization JE stays alive indefinitely while colonies exist (passive mode with hidden buttons when all 34 colonies are claimed). This means colony modifier effects still apply to the country, but display in the JE panel. Grant one only through `sr_grant_colony_modifier`, which also records it as `<modifier>_held`: a revolution's winner inherits the entry with no modifiers, and `sr_sync_solar_colonization_entry` rebuilds them from those records.
- **Localization is auto-organized** by `organize_loc.py` into category-specific files. Space race loc keys spread across `te_events_l_english.yml`, `te_journal_entries_l_english.yml`, `te_modifiers_l_english.yml`, `te_miscellaneous_l_english.yml`, and `te_concepts_l_english.yml`.

## Wonder Buildings (Construction Site Pattern)

> **Full design document:** `docs/vanilla/wonder_buildings_reference.md`

The seven **megaprojects** use a two-phase construction pattern: a buildable construction site becomes the completed building through a scripted effect. (The ordinary wonders in `common/buildings/wonders.txt` are built through the normal construction queue and are not covered here.) All seven are era-12 technologies; their sites and buildings live in `common/buildings/extra_buildings.txt`.

| Megaproject | Tech | Max level | Completed building does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Space Elevator | `space_elevator` | 20 | 1M `launch_capacity` per level, `country_space_race_progress_add` |
| Orbital Solar Collector | `space_based_solar_power` | 10 | an orbital hub: each level opens 3 `building_solar_receiver` slots (`country_solar_receiver_max_level_add`); the ground receivers produce the electricity |
| Orbital Battlestation | `orbital_weapon_platforms` | 5 | country-wide unit offense/defense/morale and nuclear-defence chance; no goods output |
| Antimatter Containment Facility | `antimatter_production` | 10 | each level opens 5 slots (`country_antimatter_facility_max_level_add`) for `building_antimatter_engine` and `building_antimatter_warhead_plant` |
| Mind Upload Nexus | `mind_backups` | 5 | digital assets, services, tourism and fine art output, research speed |
| Nanofabrication Center | `molecular_assemblers` | 10 | `advanced_materials` output, lower space race risk |
| Consciousness Network | `telepathic_communities` | 10 | state infrastructure, tax capacity and institution impact, bureaucracy; a mode group picks Open (SoL, innovation, influence) or Social Control (authority, less turmoil; unlocked by the laws that disallow Open) |

- **Construction effect:** `generic_wonder_construction_base = { WONDER = <name> MAX_LEVEL = <n> }` in `common/scripted_effects/extra_effects.txt`, called through one named wrapper per megaproject (`space_elevator_construction`, …). Each megaproject's `<name>_on_action` runs it from `on_monthly_pulse_state` (`common/on_actions/extra_on_actions.txt`). On completion it removes the site and creates the building at level 1, or raises it one level up to the cap; a site can't be placed once the building is at its cap.
- **Construction progress:** each month, `building_annual_<name>_progress / 12`, and nothing while the site has a goods shortage. The PM puts that modifier in `workforce_scaled`, so it already scales with staffing and progress is linear in occupancy; until 2026-10-01 the tick multiplied by `occupancy` again, so a half-staffed site ran at a quarter speed. The site's `<name>_progress` modifier mirrors the state's `<name>_progress_var` (`multiplier = root.var:…` from the state pulse). Speed PMs set the annual progress: paused 0, slow 0.25, medium 0.5, fast 1.0 (about 4 / 2 / 1 years at full occupancy).
- **Custom modifier types:** `building_annual_*_progress` and `building_total_*_progress` (script_only) in `megastructure_progress_modifier_types.txt`.

## Grand Monuments (Political Instrument)

A government raises a Grand Monument to what it stands for, and the next government deals with its legacy. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-grand-monument-rework-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-27-grand-monument-rework.md`. v2: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-grand-monuments-v2-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-grand-monuments-v2-phases-1-3.md`. Built: phase 1 (1,000 construction a level; vanity and Rededicate a tenth), phase 2 (commissions, names, typed names) and phase 3 (the monument policy; the owner swapped it ahead of the 1836 monuments). Not built: phase 4 (monuments standing in 1836), 5 (trophies), 6 (historical commissions). **Add a dedication:** add its row to `DEDICATIONS` in `test_grand_monument_registry.py`; the failures are the checklist. **Add a commission source, form, namesake or policy:** its row in `COMMISSION_SOURCES`, `FORMS`, `NAMESAKES` or `POLICIES` in the same file.

- **Files:** `common/buildings/grand_monuments.txt`, `common/production_methods/grand_monument_pms.txt`, `common/production_method_groups/grand_monument_pmgs.txt`, `common/scripted_triggers/monument_triggers.txt`, `common/scripted_effects/gm_effects.txt`, `common/script_values/gm_values.txt`, `common/static_modifiers/gm_modifiers.txt`, `common/journal_entries/je_grand_monuments.txt`, `gui/journal_entry_widgets/grand_monuments_widget.gui`, the state view's card in `gui/te_state_panel_widgets.gui` (`te_state_gm_card`, instanced from `gui/states_panel.gui`), `common/scripted_guis/gm_sguis.txt`, `common/customizable_localization/gm_custom_loc.txt`, `common/on_actions/monument_events_on_actions.txt`, `events/monument_events.txt`, `events/te_debug_monuments_events.txt`; the shared `common/scripted_triggers/te_heritage_triggers.txt`; `grand_monuments_rule` in `extra_game_rules.txt`. v2: `gm_commission_triggers.txt`, `gm_commission_effects.txt`, `gm_commission_values.txt`, `gm_name_triggers.txt`, `gm_name_effects.txt`, `gm_policy_triggers.txt`, `gm_policy_effects.txt`; the typed-name carrier `common/company_types/gm_name_carrier_company.txt` with `common/buildings/gm_name_carrier.txt` and its PM/PMG; founding years seeded from `common/history/extra_history.txt`.
- **Grandeur and the curve.** A monument's grandeur is its level (uncapped). Every effect grows in steps, each costing twice the grandeur of the last (`gm_curve_steps_f5` / `_f10`, eight terms; `gm_curve_next_*` for the JE's "next step"). Each static modifier carries **one step** and the multiplier is the step count; `gm_step_*` script values mirror them for loc, pinned equal by the registry test.
- **PMs carry only staff.** The dedication PMs keep the self-reference ratchet (see the file header) and `unscaled` employment; no `country_modifiers`, `state_modifiers` or goods. A new building starts on the dedication most of its owner's monuments carry, not on `is_default` (2026-10-08). The ratchet binds the panel only: `activate_production_method` passes it, so the ceremony's answer replaces that copy with one switch (`gm_state_dedicate` skips a switch to the active method; leaving it undedicated runs `gm_state_undedicate`).
- **Where modifiers live.** Local effects (`gm_local_*`) are state modifiers refreshed by `gm_state_monthly` from `on_monthly_pulse_state`. National effects (`gm_national_*`, `gm_ig_approval_<ig>`) sit on `je:je_grand_monuments`, applied by `gm_country_refresh` with ROOT = the country (monthly, or `monument_events.20` after `on_law_activated`, `on_new_ruler`, `on_state_owner_change` and a finished level, whose ROOTs are not the country). A variable backing a multiplier is zeroed, never removed.
- **JE activation.** `is_shown_when_inactive` auto-activates the entry for new saves the moment a country owns a Grand Monument (gated on `grand_monuments_rule` too). Because a new auto-activating JE never reaches a save that predates it, `gm_country_refresh` also calls `add_journal_entry` explicitly whenever the rule is on, a monument is owned and the entry is missing, so old saves and any save where the entry lapsed pick it back up on the next monthly pulse.
- **Kinds and fit.** Regime (Crown, Republic, Revolution), ruler (Leader), faith (Shrine), timeless (the rest). Fit is read live each month (`gm_state_message_fits`). Records live on the **state** (buildings hold no variables): `gm_raised_by` (a bound monument fits only while that country owns it), `gm_honoree` / `gm_honoree_ig`, `gm_faith`, `gm_skin`. `gm_state_first_sight` writes them for panel picks and old saves; after that a missing record reads as "does not fit".
- **National totals** (`gm_compute_totals`): standing grandeur (every monument not contested: prestige, cultural pull), regime grandeur (fitting regime and ruler monuments: legitimacy), one per dedication with a national effect, and one signed total per IG (approvals minus oppositions, plus its ledger, minus contested monuments whose "new order" it is), each through the curve on its absolute value. `gm_zero_ledgers`: when the last monument is gone and every ledger is idle, the ledgers are zeroed before the pulse stops.
- **Contests** (`gm_check_contests`, skipped while the country has `te_cw_role`): a bound monument that stops fitting records `gm_base_ig` / `gm_supporter_ig` flags and becomes contested; one that fits again is restored. `gm_state_changed_hands` contests a conquered regime or ruler monument and makes a conquered shrine heritage; a monument already contested when its state changes hands is contested afresh for the new owner. The winner of a civil war adopts the loser's monuments (`gm_repair_after_civil_war`, in `te_civil_war_on_won`). Choices: `gm_state_tear_down` / `_rededicate` (rebuilt at half level through the shared ladder, which runs to 200) / `_preserve`; each writes decaying ledgers (`gm_teardown_ledger`, `gm_ig_ledger_<ig>`), so nothing stacks. Players get `monument_events.16` (and `.17` per monument); the AI decides monthly. A monument demolished from the building panel while contested is recorded as torn down by its state pulse (`gm_state_record_teardown`, which also sets `gm_active`), and the JE's `invalid` also waits until no state still carries `gm_seen`, so that teardown is applied before the entry can go.
- **Skins** (`gm_skin`): faith skins by state religion, heritage skins by the language-reform revival partition (`te_heritage_<language>`, also called by `extra_law_events.25`), chosen in `monument_events.11`; `gm_inscription` names a revived language. The choice event is offered to players only; the AI keeps the most specific skin.
- **The dedication ceremony** (`monument_events.2`, a state event) starts through `gm_state_start_ceremony` from two places. `monument_events.1` starts it on a finished level of an undedicated monument. The refresh (`gm_state_offer_missed_ceremony`) starts it for an undedicated monument whose grandeur has risen above `gm_ceremony_level`, the grandeur the last ceremony asked at, or that no ceremony has asked about. The refresh path exists because five queued levels finishing on one day fired no `on_building_built` (2026-10-07, `scripting_best_practices.md`). Both also start it for a monument on a dedication nothing recorded (`gm_state_reset_copied_dedication`: no `gm_seen`, no ceremony asked), which is the engine's copy of its siblings' dedication. The event opens for any monument without `gm_seen`, and its answer is the only switch. First sight, default naming and the contest check skip a monument whose ceremony is pending, which still reads as its copy. `te_debug_monuments.3` re-opens one monument's dedication from the console. Leaving a monument undedicated clears `gm_ceremony_pending`, and the recorded level holds the next ask back until the monument grows.
- **Vanity backlash:** `monument_events.1` (a level finished, ROOT = the building) calls `gm_state_vanity_backlash` in hard times (`gm_country_hard_times`): radicals, and the linear `gm_vanity_ledger`.
- **Anniversaries:** `.3`–`.10`, `.12`–`.15`, dispatched by `monument_events_on_action` for a fitting monument of level 3 or more; every option is positive, money the only cost.
- **NOT in `bg_monuments`, and that is load-bearing.** `tourism_throughput_from_monuments` and `cultural_pull_from_monuments` iterate `bg_monuments` and assume one-off wonders. `bg_grand_monuments` is a top-level group with no `parent_group`; Grand Monument cultural pull is `cultural_pull_from_grand_monuments` (standing grandeur through the curve, at most +5).
- **Panel** (`grand_monuments_widget.gui`, style pass 2026-09-29): three named roots, each a gated wrapper round a composer type, so the Timeline Extended window's tab reuses the sections. Container 1 holds the overview: four status cells (Upheld, Heritage, Undedicated, Contested; the middle two words are the concepts `concept_heritage_monument` / `concept_contested_monument`), lit while their live count (`gm_disp_count_*`) is above zero, and under them, only while `gm_hard_times_sgui` holds, the Hard Times icon over a red "Hard Times" in a fixed 480 column. The entry has no status description: its counts were the overview's. Container 2 holds National Effects (open, `gm_national_closed`) and Our Monuments (open, `gm_monuments_closed`). In National Effects each total shows only while in force, with an empty-state line gated on `gm_disp_national_any`, and each stepped total is a bar filled by `gm_disp_frac_*`, which is (2G − N + f) / (N + f) from its `gm_g_*` / `gm_n_*` pair, with the grandeur and the next step on hover. Container 3 holds How Grand Monuments Work (collapsed, `gm_how_open`). Everything is display-only; `test_grand_monuments_layout.py` pins the order, the flags, the gates and each bar's f. The status and Hard Times icons are the mod's own, from PR #586 (`gfx/interface/icons/gm_icons/`, listed in `docs/systems/grand_monuments_gui_icons.md`).
- **Timeline Extended window (2026-09-30; not yet seen in game).** The same three composers also show on the *Grand Monuments* tab of the mod's own window (`gui/te_systems_window.gui`), under `datacontext = "[GetPlayerJournalEntry('je_grand_monuments')]"`, then a link back to the entry. The tab is greyed until the entry runs; its tooltip lists `gm_entry_unlocked` (owning a Grand Monument).
- **State view card (2026-10-09; not yet seen in game).** `te_state_gm_card`, a 530 px card under the tourism card, shown while `gm_status_code` is above 0 in any country's state. The row's status icon and word (`gm_row_status`), title (`gm_row_title` / `_named`), contest line and inscription, then Grandeur with a bar of its own step (f = 5: `gm_state_disp_next_step`, `gm_state_disp_step_pct`, from the live level), its Tourism Industry throughput (`gm_state_disp_tourism`) and its dedication's local effect (`gm_state_disp_local`, steps × `gm_step_local_<key>`), each from the steps `gm_state_monthly` last wrote and hidden at 0. The local effect's name and format are the custom localization `gm_local_effect_label` / `_value`, one branch per dedication (`StateCardTests` in the registry test). Display only: the player's own state gets a goto, gated on `te_window_grand_monuments_tab_sgui`, that opens the Timeline Extended window on its Grand Monuments tab (two `onclick` lines: `te_systems_window_tab` to `grand_monuments`, then `com_open_window`).
- **Commissions (v2 §2).** One record per country in country variables (`gm_commission_effects.txt` header lists them); the petitioner is a flag naming its type (a civil war's winner has other IG objects). Offered by `gm_com_send_offer` through `monument_events.21` (the same event for the AI; `gm_com_ai_declines`), open in the JE's Commission section, closed by `gm_com_fulfil` / `_miss` / `_lapse` / `_decline`, all through `gm_com_close` (60-month petition cooldown). Progress is `gm_com_progress`: fitting grandeur of the dedication (in the fixed state, if any) above the baseline at acceptance, floored at 0, checked by every `gm_country_refresh` (before the totals) and the monthly tick. Sources: the world pass `gm_com_world_on_action` (every country, monthly: the ruler and how long they have reigned, founding year, regime edge, space firsts, January centenaries, the 1-in-120 petition roll), `on_won_war` / `on_lost_war` (1.14.5: `scope:war`, `scope:enemy_country`, `scope:benefitted_from_wargoal` / `victim_of_wargoal`; capitulations included; gated on `is_warleader = root` and `war_duration_months >= 12`), `on_character_death` (a ruler of 180 months), `on_country_formed` (founding year). The regime tracker writes nothing on a side in a civil war, so a winning revolution inherits the nation's last regime and its edge is seen. A counted monument that drops out lowers the baseline (it stops counting rather than cancelling later progress). A petition, which asks only for the city's name, does not rename a monument that already has one. Great and major powers only (`gm_com_can_offer`); a commission runs `gm_com_time_months`, fixed on acceptance: 60 months for a great power, 120 for a major one. A miss costs the petitioner more than a refusal (−15 against −5), and an unanswered offer is declined. One open or offered at a time; a world moment while busy is dropped. A stuck offer is forgotten after six months (`gm_com_offer_watch`). The AI accepts by `ai_chance` and queues the levels with `start_building_construction` in a `while` (one level a call); its ceremony at a monument that can answer the commission takes the commission's dedication for certain (every other option ×0). The country pulse and the JE run while a commission is open or offered; `gm_entry_unlocked` too. The promise ledger (`gm_national_promise`, linear) joins v1's ledger lists. No offer to a side in a civil war; a revolutionary country never writes its own founding year. Countries of 1836 have a founding year only from `gm_seed_founding_years` (no 1936 centenary flood).
- **Names (v2 §3).** Records on the state beside `gm_skin` (`gm_name_effects.txt` header), all cleared by `gm_clear_state`. Forms by skin are `gm_state_form_fits_<form>` (the plan's Task 7 table fixes the spec's §3.2 draft; the five civic-progress dedications have their building's form, faith skins their building's name). The unveiling: `gm_state_offer_skins` → (players, more than one skin) `.11`, else `gm_unveil_pending` for the next refresh (the unveiling reads the production method the dedication has just activated, which may not be visible in the same effect) → `gm_state_unveil` sets the default form and namesake (the commission's when `gm_state_answers_commission`) → (players) `.24` form, only when the skin offers more than one → `.25` namesake; each event's default keeps what is set. A fulfilled commission renames the monument that completed it unless it was unveiled during that commission (`gm_unveiled_for_com`). Names print through `gm_monument_name_row` (`State`) and `gm_monument_name_evt` (`SCOPE.sState('monument_state')`): customizable-localization targets inherit the caller's context, so each pattern exists once per context. An option name loses the event's saved scopes inside a target, so `.25`'s keep option splices the `_evt` pattern keys itself, one triggered name per branch of the family. Old monuments are named by the refresh (`gm_state_name_if_unnamed`). Rename (`gm_rename_sgui`) reruns `.24`/`.25` for an upheld monument.
- **Typed names (v2 §3.5).** Name It adds `company_gm_name_carrier` and a company slot, the row's naming line (`gm_naming_line`) opens the company-rename popup from its `_show` state and a 1×1 watcher sends `gm_name_use_sgui` when the company's name changes; the name is stored as the flag `gm_name` and the row title prints it straight (`gm_row_title_named`). `gm_name_carrier_close` ends a naming; `gm_name_sweep` closes one abandoned for a year (game time runs while the box is open; the clock restarts as the naming line appears), and the country pulse keeps running while `gm_naming_active` even with no monument left. A state that changes hands drops `gm_being_named`. Every `Var('gm_name_carrier')` read sits in the naming line. Keep `building_gm_name_carrier_anchor` forever: saves list it.
- **Monument policy (v2 §5).** `gm_policy` flag (`gm_policy_effects.txt`); factor script values `gm_policy_factor_standing` / `_regime` (on the step counts after the curve in `gm_compute_totals`, `gm_policy_scale_steps`, so the effect moves by the factor; scaling grandeur before the curve would take off about one step) and `_tourism` / `_local` (on the steps in `gm_state_monthly`, now split into `gm_local_tourism_steps` and `gm_local_steps`); upkeep through `gm_policy_upkeep_open` / `_mothballed`, `goods_input_construction_mult` ±0.5 on the monument building itself (the maintenance input is `level_scaled`, which throughput does not touch; the engine's `pm_retooling` scales a building's construction input the same way). 60-month cooldown after any change but the first. Anniversaries: the dispatcher skips Mothballed and halves its do-nothing weight under Open. AI each January (`gm_policy_ai_choose`).
- **Debug:** `event te_debug_monuments.1`; `event te_debug_monuments.2` (offer each commission source, meet/miss/lapse the open one); `TE_MONUMENTS:` lines each month for players.

## Temporary Amendments (Sunset Clauses)

Three amendments can be attached **temporarily** (`add_amendment = { … timeout = <months> }`) as enactment concessions. The engine removes them when the timeout (counted from law activation) elapses and fires `on_amendment_timeout`, which `common/on_actions/amendment_on_actions.txt` routes to a follow-up event offering to make the clause permanent (re-add without `timeout`; `te_legitimacy_drain` + radicals) or let it lapse (Industrialist disapproval + a few capitalist radicals). Engine semantics and authoring rules: `docs/guides/scripting_best_practices.md` § Temporary amendments.

| Amendment | Delivery (DEBATE checkpoint) | cooldown / timeout (months) | Expiry event |
|-----------|------------------------------|-----------------------------|--------------|
| `amendment_env_grandfather_clause` (new; +5% pollution, +5% emissions, +2 Industrialist approval on `law_ministry_of_the_environment`; cancels one institution level) | `ministry_law_events.58` option a | 48 / 120 | `ministry_law_events.59` |
| `amendment_national_champion_exemption` | `extra_law_events.29` option e (option a remains the permanent variant) | 48 / 120 | `extra_law_events.85` |
| `amendment_corporate_data_exemption` | `extra_law_events.31` option e (option a remains the permanent variant) | 12 / 36 | `extra_law_events.86` |

The `has_amendment` guards on events 29/31/58 make the permanent and temporary variants mutually exclusive within one enactment. The expiry events re-derive `sunset_law` from `active_law:<lawgroup>` and `industrialists_ig` in `immediate`, and are not in any checkpoint pool. Deferred from issue #278: a financial-regulation phase-in (the three laws' penalties are structurally different — numeric, none, boolean lock) and a wartime rules-of-war clause (no per-country war-start on-action; would need a timeout on an already-active law).

## Amendment Petitions (`amendment_petition_events`)

Rare events in which an interest group asks for one of the mod's amendments on a law **already in force**, because the country's situation gives it a case. The enactment events (`extra_law_events.28`–`.34`, `.87`–`.90`, `inheritance_events.10`–`.13`, `ministry_law_events.58`) offer amendments only while a law is debated; these reach a law years after it passed. One event per amendment, two options: attach it (option a) or decline (option b, the default).

- **Files**: `events/amendment_petition_events.txt`, `common/scripted_triggers/amendment_petition_triggers.txt` (every `te_ap_*` trigger), `common/on_actions/amendment_petition_on_actions.txt`, loc in `te_events_l_english.yml`.
- **Frequency**: each event rolls on its own, from a named sub-action of `on_yearly_pulse_country` (`te_amendment_petition_<amendment>`) whose `random_events` pairs a no-event weight (`N = 0`, the `cultural_hegemony_events_on_actions.txt` idiom) with the event, the two summing to 1000. The event's weight is its yearly chance in tenths of a percent while the country qualifies (the Chance column below), and a country that doesn't qualify sees nothing however the engine treats a failing event. The chance follows how long the situation lasts: lasting ones (a multicultural population, a dominant governing group) roll every year they hold, so they roll low; a war or an election campaign rolls high. Every event's trigger also asks `te_ap_window_open`: each petition sets the country variable `te_ap_recent_petition` for five years in its `immediate`, whatever the answer, so a country sees at most one petition every five years. Each event also has its own 20-year cooldown (`very_long_modifier_time`), so a declined petition doesn't come back soon.
- **Law qualification**: `te_ap_law_ok_<amendment>` holds when a law the amendment allows is in force (the amendment's `allowed_laws`, restated; keep the two in step), the law doesn't carry the amendment yet, and no law of its group is being enacted (`te_ap_enacting_in_<group>` lists every law in the group, variants included; a law added to one of these groups belongs in its list). It is also each event's `cancellation_trigger`. The trigger restates the amendment's `possible` too, because `add_amendment` silently does nothing when `possible` fails.
- **Attachment**: `scope:petition_law` (saved from `active_law:<group>`) gets `add_amendment = { type sponsor = scope:petition_sponsor_ig cooldown = 60 }`: permanent, unrepealable for five years. No timeout: a timeout on an active law is unverified (§ Temporary Amendments).
- **Reactions**: option a gives the sponsor `ig_approval_positive_modifier` and the group that opposes the amendment `ig_approval_negative_modifier` (five years, decaying), plus radicals among the pops it hurts; option b gives the sponsor `ig_approval_negative_modifier` and radicals among its supporters or the pops it would have helped. AI weights favour accepting when the sponsor is in government or powerful, and declining when the opposing group is in government.

| Event | Chance | Amendment | Host law in force | Situation | Sponsor | Option a angers |
|-------|--------|-----------|-------------------|-----------|---------|-----------------|
| `.1` The Old Letters | 5% | Minority Script Preservation | State-Led Language Reform, Civic Monolingualism | `te_ap_minority_restive` (separatists growing, or a cultural minority movement at 5%+); a state with a non-primary culture | Intelligentsia | Armed Forces; discriminated pops loyal |
| `.2` Spoken Like a Citizen | 2.5% | Language Requirement for Naturalization | Cultural, Assimilatory or Universal Citizenship, beside Civic Monolingualism, State-Led Language Reform or Linguistic Purity | `te_ap_nativist_pressure` (cultural majority movement at 5%+, or primary cultures under 70%) | Petite Bourgeoisie, else Rural Folk | Intelligentsia; discriminated pops radical |
| `.3` A Second Official Tongue | 5% | Official Bilingualism | Multilingual Federalism, State-Led Language Reform | `te_ap_minority_large_and_restive` (primary cultures under 80% and the minority restive) | Intelligentsia, else Trade Unions | bureaucrats radical |
| `.4` Too Large to Break | 10% | National Champion Exemption | Antitrust Enforcement, Regulated Utilities | a company, and `te_ap_economy_faltering` (banking-cycle panic or downturn, losing power rank, or default) | Industrialists | Petite Bourgeoisie; shopkeepers radical |
| `.5` The Workers' Own Firms | 5% | Worker Cooperative Preference | Antitrust Enforcement, Regulated Utilities | labor movement at 5%+, Trade Unions powerful or governing | Trade Unions | Industrialists; capitalists radical |
| `.6` Order in the Courts | 10% | Mandatory Minimum Sentences | Restorative or Rehabilitation-Focused Criminal Justice | `te_ap_unrest` (country turmoil over 20% or radicals over 15%) | Petite Bourgeoisie, else Armed Forces | Intelligentsia; academics radical. Option b also `te_legitimacy_drain` (2.5 years) |
| `.7` The Cost of Cells | 5% | Community Service Alternative | Punishment-Focused Criminal Justice | debt at 25%+ of the ceiling, or default | Intelligentsia, else Trade Unions | Petite Bourgeoisie |
| `.8` The Data Lobby | 10% | Corporate Data Access Exemption | Moderate Data Privacy, Strong Privacy Rights | `te_ap_economy_faltering`, Industrialists powerful or governing | Industrialists | Intelligentsia; academics radical |
| `.9` Those Who Speak Up | 5% | Whistleblower Protection Act | Moderate Data Privacy, Strong Privacy Rights | legitimacy under 40 | Intelligentsia | the strongest other governing group. Option b also `te_legitimacy_drain` (5 years) |
| `.10` Never the First | 5% | No-First-Strike Pledge | Humanitarian Regulations, Limited War | nuclear rule on, Nuclear Weapons researched, at peace, a nuclear-armed rival or an anti-war movement at 5%+; `te_ap_no_first_strike_doctrine_fits` | Intelligentsia, else Trade Unions | Armed Forces; officers radical |
| `.11` Necessity Knows No Law | 25% | Military Necessity Clause | War Crimes Forbidden, Humanitarian Regulations | at war, Armed Forces powerful or governing | Armed Forces | Intelligentsia; academics radical |
| `.12` Pennies Against Millions | 25% | Small Donor Matching | Donation Limits, Publicly Funded Elections | election campaign, Industrialists or Landowners powerful | Trade Unions, else Intelligentsia | Industrialists |
| `.13` The Pulpit's Exception | 5% | Religious Exemption Clause | Basic Protections, Anti-Discrimination Laws, Full Equality and Protection | Devout powerful or governing, and below zero approval | Devout | Intelligentsia; academics radical. Option b gives the Devout `ig_approval_very_negative_modifier` |
| `.14` Rotation in Office | 2.5% | Spoils System | Appointed or Elected Bureaucrats | a governing group with at least half the government's strength | that group (most clout in government) | bureaucrats and academics radical |
| `.15` Company Men | 5% | Corporate Security Powers | Local, Dedicated or Militarized Police Force | labor movement at 5%+ or `te_ap_unrest`; the amendment's `possible` | Industrialists | Trade Unions; lower strata radical |
| `.16` Complaints Against the Force | 5% | Civilian Oversight | Local or Dedicated Police Force, Private Policing | civil rights movement at 5%+ or `te_ap_unrest`; Civil Rights Movement researched | Intelligentsia | Armed Forces; bureaucrats radical, discriminated pops loyal |
| `.17` What the Dead Owe | 2.5%, +25% at war | Estate Duties | Primogeniture & Entail, Customary, Forced Heirship, Freedom of Testation, without the Birthright Endowment | at war, or a reformer or social democrat group in government (`te_inh_reformers_in_government`); Political Agitation | Intelligentsia, else Trade Unions | Landowners; aristocrats radical |
| `.18` Dynasty Trusts | 2.5% | Perpetual Dynastic Trusts | Freedom of Testation | Industrialists powerful or governing; Globalization | Industrialists | Trade Unions; lower strata radical |
| `.19` The Duty and the Stake | 2.5% | Birthright Endowment (replaces Estate Duties, `te_inh_swap_duties_for_endowment`) | a law with private estates carrying Estate Duties | a reformer or social democrat group in government; Universal Basic Income | Trade Unions, else Intelligentsia | Industrialists |
| `.20` The Farm Is Not to Be Cut | 2.5% | Undivided Farm Succession | Forced Heirship, Customary | Rural Folk powerful or governing | Rural Folk | Landowners; peasants radical |

- **The No-First-Strike Pledge** binds the deterrence doctrine to No First Use while in force (§ Nuclear Deterrence and Crisis Diplomacy). `te_ap_no_first_strike_doctrine_fits` offers it only where that changes nothing: no posture yet (`nd_doctrine` unset; an arsenal built later starts under the pledge) or a doctrine already at 1. Option a's tooltip (`amendment_petition_events.10.a.tt`) says so.
- **Not covered**: the generated tax-code amendments (tax bills only), the language-reform choice amendments (events `extra_law_events.25`–`.27` and their repeal loop), the Collective Governance amendments (script-managed) and the Environmental Grandfather Clause (a phase-in at enactment, with its own expiry event).
- **Adding a petition** for a new amendment: a `te_ap_law_ok_<amendment>` trigger (and a `te_ap_enacting_in_<group>` list if the group is new), an event of the same shape, its own `te_amendment_petition_<amendment>` sub-action with a chance (`amendment_petition_on_actions.txt`), loc, the picture's `events` entry in `scripts/image_pipeline/event_image_prompts.py`, a row above and in the player guide's petition list (`docs/player_guide/20-appendix-reference-lists.md`).

## Legislated Tax Code (`je_tax_code`, `te_tax_code_rule`)

**Experimental, default off.** Under the rule the five vanilla taxation laws cannot be enacted; every country holds the carrier `law_te_tax_code`, whose generated amendments set each rate, and taxes change only through a bill that interest groups must commit to, taking effect on the 1st of its month. The customs option legislates a market owner's tariffs too. AI countries legislate through the same commands (package 6, the schema's "AI legislation"): a staggered monthly step raises taxes on a fiscal-deficit streak, cuts them on a surplus streak, passes emergency bills, bargains with the same offers and keeps or renegotiates its promises; their tariffs stay native until the customs probe (P09b/P09c) decides the mechanism. The UI is the journal entry and the Budget panel's Tax Code tab.

- **Design of record**: [`tax_code_schema.md`](tax_code_schema.md): tokens and sentinels, the one collection writer, the scheduler, migration, civil wars, drift, the bill lifecycle and support model, offers, obligations, the economy snapshot and fiscal record, the panels and customs. Read it before touching any `te_tax_*` variable.
- **Engine capabilities and play-tests**: [`../testing/tax-code-capability-ledger.md`](../testing/tax-code-capability-ledger.md): what is verified in game and what is only read statically (row 29 lists the unproven engine forms the panels rely on), the items recorded for later, the evidence matrix and the known limitations. The play-test runbook is [`../testing/tax-code-playtest.md`](../testing/tax-code-playtest.md).
- **Spec and plan**: [`../superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-legislated-tax-code-design.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-legislated-tax-code-design.md) and, for the AI and the release, [`../superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-tax-code-ai-and-release-design.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-tax-code-ai-and-release-design.md); plans [`../superpowers/plans/2026-10-02-legislated-tax-code-tasks.md`](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-02-legislated-tax-code-tasks.md) and [`../superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-tax-code-ai-and-release-tasks.md`](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-tax-code-ai-and-release-tasks.md).
- **Generated files** come only from `scripts/generators/gen_tax_code.py` (`--check` in the tests; [`../auto_generated_files.md`](../auto_generated_files.md)). Tests: `test_tax_code_*.py`.

## Collective Governance (`law_direct_democracy`)

The governance principle `law_direct_democracy` is displayed as **Collective Governance**: no individual holds supreme executive power. The key is historical (the law was Direct Democracy) and kept because renaming a law breaks saves. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-collective-governance-design.md`.

- **What it means depends on Distribution of Power**, expressed as one amendment on the law per group: the voting laws → *Direct Democracy* (the old referendum package, with a soft movement gate), Single-Party State → *Collective Leadership*, Technocracy → *Collegial Administration*, Anarchy → *Free Federation*, Oligarchy/Organic Regulation → *Patrician Council*, Algorithmic Governance → *Algorithmic Commons*. Autocracy can't hold the law. The law itself carries only the "no head" base (legitimacy from broad coalitions, less authority, slower enactment).
- **Soft movement gate (2026-10).** Direct Democracy and Free Federation used to carry `country_must_have_movement_to_enact_laws_bool`, which blocks every law no movement backs. Four mod law groups (Privacy Rights, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Consumer Protection, Urban Planning) have no movement stance at all, and the likeliest reading of "backs" is comparative (the movement rates the law above the current one), so under Anarchy the anarchist movement, already content, backs nothing and every law and exit needed a movement hostile to the regime. Both now carry a flat `country_law_enactment_success_add = -0.1` instead. A movement backing a law adds its support to the law's success chance natively (`LAW_ENACTMENT_MOVEMENT_SUPPORT_PASS_EFFECT = 1.0`; its activism can't drop below the 25% it needs while the law is enacted), so backed laws make up the 10% and unbacked laws pass slowly, or can't start under 11% success from the interest groups (`LAW_MIN_SUCCESS_CHANCE = 0.01`). Direct Democracy's +25% success went with the gate. Free Federation adds `state_loyalists_from_political_movements_mult = 0.5` (25% → 37.5% of a backing movement's supporters turn loyal on passage, `MOVEMENT_LOYALISTS_ON_APPROVED_ENACTMENT`) and `country_bolster_cost_mult = -0.5` (offsets Anarchy's −50% authority for bolstering). `test_no_movement_gate` keeps the bool off the law and every amendment. No modifier can make the gate per law: `political_movement_enact_support_mult` exists only in vanilla loc, and the movement-support defines are global.
- **Script-attached, never negotiated.** `te_refresh_collective_governance_amendment` (`collective_governance_effects.txt`) keeps exactly the matching amendment, derived from current laws on every call: the law's `on_activate` (hidden), `on_law_activated` (after the consistency cascade) and the monthly country pulse. The amendments' `possible` and `can_repeal` ask the same triggers (`collective_governance_triggers.txt`), so the player can't repeal the one their Distribution of Power calls for.
- **Preview:** the law's `on_enact` names the amendment the current Distribution of Power will give (the language-reform pattern).
- **Government types** (`timeline_extended_governments.txt`) run most specific first, because the first match wins: Noble Commonwealth (Oligarchy + Feudal Contracts) before Patrician Council, with `gov_collective_governance` as the catch-all. Algorithmic Commons comes before `gov_algorithmic_directorate`, which keys on Algorithmic Governance alone.
- **"Democracy" readings** (the liberal-democratic cultural-hegemony model, the referendum events `extra_law_events.24/.60/.84`) also require `collective_governance_is_popular`.
- **Adding a Distribution of Power law:** add it to its row of `EXPRESSIONS` in `test_collective_governance.py` (or add a row); the failures are the checklist.

## Bureaucracy Laws: Who Staffs the Administration (`law_contracted_administration`, `law_communal_administration`, `amendment_spoils_system`)

Beside vanilla's Hereditary, Appointed and Elected Bureaucrats and Automated Bureaucracy (next section), two human-staffed bureaucracy laws (`common/laws/extra_laws.txt`) and one amendment (`common/amendments/extra_amendments.txt`). Each law's identity is who staffs the Government Administrations: its own PM in vanilla's `pmg_government_administration_bureaucrat_professionalism`, which replaces Professional Bureaucrats while the law holds (`INJECT:pm_professional_bureaucrats` `disallowing_laws` lists all three mod laws), plus clout. They avoid Neocameralism's and Devolution's levers, so they stack with those laws without doubling up. Spec §7–12: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-bureaucracy-laws-design.md`.

- **Contracted Administration** (Supply Chain Management; `unlocking_laws` Laissez-Faire or Interventionism; disallowed by Council Republic and Algorithmic Governance): capitalists +15% and bureaucrats −25% political strength, +25% institution size change speed, +5% institution bureaucracy cost (contract overruns), no Legislative Override Capacity. PM `pm_service_contractors`: 250 bureaucrats a level plus 20 Services, a local good, so a state with few Urban Centers pays more and can go short.
- **Communal Administration** (no technology; `unlocking_laws` Council Republic, Anarchy or Collective Governance; disallowed by Technocracy, Elder Council and Algorithmic Governance, Elected's vanilla exclusions plus machine rule): bureaucrats −25%, Trade Unions and Rural Folk +10% political strength, −5% movement radicalism, −10% tax capacity, −2 Legislative Override Capacity. PM `pm_recallable_delegates`: 250 bureaucrats and 250 clerks a level.
- **Progressiveness and the consistency walk.** Contracted is −2: below 0 keeps Appointed as Elected's fallback, and above −5 makes Appointed, not Automated (−10), Contracted's own (−5 ties and goes to the ideology tiebreak). Communal is 75, so a Council Republic or Collective Governance that loses Elected lands on Communal, and losing Communal falls back to Elected, else Appointed. Re-read `te_fix_inconsistent_lawgroup_bureaucracy` after changing any bureaucracy law's progressiveness or constraints.
- **Spoils System** (`amendment_spoils_system`): parent Appointed, allowed on Appointed and Elected (the US starts with Elected), −10% tax capacity, sponsor +15% political strength. Sponsored by IGs in government or led by a Jacksonian Democrat, and offered by `extra_law_events.87` ("To the Victor Belong the Spoils", debate checkpoint) while either law is enacted, sponsored by the governing IG with the most clout. Vanilla's `amendment_preserved_bureaucratic_caste` is the template and is left unchanged.
- **Adding a bureaucracy law:** `disallowing_laws = { law_algorithmic_governance }` (or machine rule evicts it), a progressiveness checked against the cascade, its PM in the professionalism group plus the `pm_professional_bureaucrats` INJECT, Appointed's two IG-shift `on_enact` tooltips, an inline Legislative Override Capacity value or a comment saying why none, ideology stances, loc, a law icon (a `law` entry in `scripts/image_pipeline/icon_prompts.py`), the player guide.

## Machine Administration and Machine Rule (`law_automated_bureaucracy`, `law_algorithmic_governance`)

Bureaucracy decides who administers the state; Distribution of Power decides who rules it. **Automated Bureaucracy** (Bureaucracy, Generative AI) hands administration to machines under human authority, and any government can hold it. **Algorithmic Governance** (Distribution of Power, Artificial Intelligence) hands the machines the authority itself. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-bureaucracy-laws-design.md`. The human-staffed laws beside it are in the Bureaucracy Laws section above.

- **Automated Bureaucracy:** −20% institution bureaucracy cost, +50% institution size change speed, −25% bureaucrat and +15% engineer political strength, +1 Legislative Override Capacity. Progressiveness −10, so `gen_law_consistency` still moves a country that loses Elected Bureaucrats to Appointed. Its staffing is `pm_automated_casework` in vanilla's `pmg_government_administration_bureaucrat_professionalism`: 150 bureaucrats and 100 engineers a level instead of 500 bureaucrats, plus Digital Access and Digital Assets. That PM replaces Professional Bureaucrats while the law holds (`disallowing_laws` INJECTed on `pm_professional_bureaucrats`), as vanilla's Hereditary Bureaucrats PM does.
- **Install and lock.** Algorithmic Governance's `on_activate` enacts Automated Bureaucracy. Appointed and Hereditary Bureaucrats (and the Crownland Diets variant) get `disallowing_laws = { law_algorithmic_governance }` (`common/laws/modified.txt`), Contracted and Communal Administration carry it in their own definitions, and Elected needs a voting law, so Automated is the only valid bureaucracy law under machine rule. Don't put `unlocking_laws = { law_automated_bureaucracy }` on Algorithmic Governance: Distribution of Power is fixed before Bureaucracy in the consistency walk, so any bureaucracy change would evict the governing law, and old saves would lose it.
- **Algorithmic Governance** keeps no administrative effects. Its legitimacy is a flat `country_legitimacy_base_add = 70` on the law plus `algorithmic_mandate` (`sol_expectations_modifiers.txt`): +1 base legitimacy times `algorithmic_mandate_value`, which is 5 × the cached SoL expectations gap, capped at ±40. The base is there because every other Distribution of Power law carries legitimacy of its own (clout 25–120, votes 40–110, Elder Council a flat 20) and this one has no clout, votes or head-of-state term: with the mandate alone, a Presidential Republic on very low taxes sat at 42 (Unstable Government) with the mandate at its old +25 cap, and a first fix of base 50 with the cap unchanged still topped out at 92 there. Base plus cap is sized for headroom, not for the neutral case: 70 + 40 = 110 before taxes (`tax_modifier_*`: +10 very low, +5 low, 0 medium, −10 high, −20 very high), the head of state's group and timed modifiers. At the +40 cap a Presidential Republic reaches 100 through High taxes and 97 at Very High; at +25 (a gap of 5) it reaches 100 through Medium and falls to 92 and 82. A country on a steady standard of living sits near 70 plus those terms. The tooltip on enactment (`ALGORITHMIC_GOVERNANCE_TT_MANDATE`) explains the mandate and quotes the 5 and the 40; `test_algorithmic_mandate.py` pins them to the script value. The mandate is re-applied from `sol_expectations_monthly_on_action` right after the update caches the gap; that is its only refresh site, and the law's `on_deactivate` removes it. The gap is zero once expectations catch up, so the mandate pays for improvement and costs for decline, not for a level of wealth. Together with the doubled radicals and loyalists from SoL change, a falling standard of living is what threatens machine rule.
- **Events:** `society_technology_events.27` (Generative AI) and `post_scarcity_events.3` belong to Automated Bureaucracy. `extra_law_events.83`, the dispute over who has the last word, is Automated Bureaucracy's enactment event, and `.23`/`.59` fire for either law.

## Policing: Private Policing and Its Amendments (`law_private_policing`, `amendment_corporate_security_powers`, `amendment_civilian_police_oversight`, #628)

`lawgroup_policing` answers who provides ordinary enforcement, who pays and whom it answers to. Beside vanilla's No Police, Local Police Force (public constabularies under local notables), Dedicated Police Force and Militarized Police Force, the mod adds one law (`common/laws/extra_laws.txt`) and two amendments (`common/amendments/extra_amendments.txt`). Internal Security (regime protection) and Criminal Justice (punishment and rehabilitation) are separate groups. The group's and the four vanilla laws' descriptions are overridden in `localization/english/replace/` to say who pays and answers. Every effect is aggregate: coverage that follows money is modelled within states (strata standard of living) rather than as a per-state modifier, because every state has rich and poor districts (owner call). Spec, with the decision on every candidate in the issue: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-policing-laws-design.md`.

- **Private Policing** (Computer Networks; `unlocking_laws` Laissez-Faire or Interventionism; disallowed by Council Republic). Keeps `institution_police` (displayed as Ministry of Public Safety) at −40% institution cost, vanilla Private Schools' and Private Health Insurance's value. Per investment level: upper strata +0.25 and middle strata +0.1 SoL, `state_turmoil_effects_mult` +0.05 (half the institution's own −0.1), `goods_output_services_mult` −0.01 (owners' funding: the guards are service workers, so Services buyers pay through prices; an investment-pool cut was rejected as too harsh on the corporate economies most likely to enact it). Flat: Trade Unions −10% political strength, Labor Movement pop attraction +15%, Legislative Override Capacity −0.5 (No Police's value). No IG clout bonus per level: the issue warned against moving Local Police's landowner bonus to the Industrialists.
- **Who favours it.** Only Corpocrat (strongly approve) and Corporate Rule (approve); `ai_will_do` needs a Corpocrat ruler (Neocameralism's rule). Market ideologies are left neutral. The disapproving vanilla ideologies are in `ideology_modifications.py` (`private_policing_*`). Every ideology with a Policing block states a stance on it (`ideology_lawgroup_audit`), and one the block is new to also states the four base-game police laws.
- **Consistency walk.** Progressiveness 25 equals Dedicated Police Force, so `te_fix_inconsistent_lawgroup_policing` moves a country that loses its market economy or becomes a Council Republic to Dedicated Police Force (nationalization; the shared institution keeps its investment), or No Police without Law Enforcement.
- **Corporate Security Powers:** parent Private Policing (so the same corpocrat-only stance), allowed on Local, Dedicated and Militarized Police Force; Corporate Charters and not Command Economy, Cooperative Ownership or Industry Banned; −10% police institution cost, Trade Unions −10%, Labor Movement +15%, sponsor +10% political strength. Sponsored by the Industrialists or any IG approving the parent, and offered by `extra_law_events.89` ("Guards at the Mill Gate", debate checkpoint) with the Industrialists as sponsor. Not allowed on Private Policing, which already delegates the whole force.
- **Civilian Oversight:** parent Guaranteed Liberties, allowed on Local, Dedicated and Private Policing (not Militarized); Civil Rights Movement; +10% police institution cost, Civil Rights Movement pop attraction −15%, Legislative Override Capacity −0.5. Sponsored by the Intelligentsia or any IG approving the parent, and offered by `extra_law_events.90` ("Who Watches the Watchmen", debate checkpoint) with the Intelligentsia as sponsor.

## Inheritance: Who Inherits (`lawgroup_inheritance`)

Six laws answer one question, who inherits an estate; the group's systemic effects come from script rather than from the laws' modifier blocks. Spec, with the owner's decisions of 2026-10-08: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-inheritance-laws-design.md`. Files: laws in `common/laws/extra_laws.txt` (the INHERITANCE block), amendments at the foot of `common/amendments/extra_amendments.txt`, `te_inheritance_values.txt` (script values), `te_inheritance_effects.txt`, `te_inheritance_triggers.txt`, `te_inheritance_modifiers.txt`, `te_inheritance_on_actions.txt`, `events/inheritance_events.txt`.

| Law (key) | Unlock | Concentration target | Own modifiers |
|---|---|---|---|
| Primogeniture & Entail (`law_primogeniture`) | start | 80 | radicals +10%, qualifications −10%, tax capacity −10%, bureaucracy cost −5% |
| Customary Inheritance (`law_partible`) | start; GLOBAL default (`extra_history.txt`) | 50 | none |
| Forced Heirship (`law_equal_inheritance`) | Egalitarianism | 25 | radicals −10%, Rural Folk +10%, education access +5%, tax capacity +10%, bureaucracy cost +5% |
| Freedom of Testation (`law_free_testation`) | start | 60 | capitalists' investment pool +10%, bureaucracy cost +3% |
| State as Universal Heir (`law_state_universal_heir`) | Command Economy | 0 | +200 authority, dividends efficiency +5%, Bureaucrats +10%, bureaucracy cost +5%, upper strata −20% and middle strata −10% investment-pool efficiency |
| Possession by Use (`law_non_inheritable_usage_rights`) | Cooperative Ownership | 0 | population bureaucracy cost −5%, Rural Folk and Trade Unions +10%, the same investment-pool cuts |

- **Keys are historical.** Each old key kept its meaning (Partible → Customary, Equal Inheritance → Forced Heirship, Usage Rights → Possession by Use), so saves keep their laws. The two new laws come after the old four in file order (a law at the head of an existing group broke pre-change saves, #520); the defensive `te_repair_double_inheritance_law` (`te_old_save_repairs.txt`) clears any country left holding two. A pre-redesign save's Command-Economy holder of Usage Rights moves to State as Universal Heir through the monthly consistency walk, which now finds Possession by Use unlocked by Cooperative Ownership only.
- **Starting laws**: France Forced Heirship, Britain and Japan Primogeniture & Entail, the USA Freedom of Testation, everyone else (Russia included) Customary.
- **Progressiveness** shapes `te_fix_inconsistent_lawgroup_inheritance`: State as Universal Heir 50 and Possession by Use 60 fall to each other when the economic system allows, else to Forced Heirship (25, needs Egalitarianism), else Customary (10). Freedom of Testation is 5 so it is never that fallback.
- **`ideological_opinion_impact = 0.5`** (was 0): groups react to the law in force. France's Landowners now dislike Forced Heirship from 1836; balance item to check in play.
- **Wealth-concentration score** (`te_inh_concentration`, 0–100, concept `concept_inheritance_concentration`). Kept per state since the demographics census (§ Demographics): each state holds a score `te_dg_wc` and a target `te_dg_wc_target`, and `te_inh_concentration` is the national figure, the states' average weighted by where property is held (`te_demog_wc_national`, the one refresh site of the figure and of #822's modifiers). A state's target is the law's (`te_inh_law_amendment_target`: the table above plus Estate Duties −25, Birthright Endowment −30, Perpetual Trusts +30, Undivided Farm Succession +10, clamped 0–100) plus land tenure, who owns the state's capital, its income inequality and the country's taxes on wealth, clamped 0–100, or pinned at the state's score under continuity of title. Each state's yearly pulse (`te_demog_wc_state_yearly`, straight after its walk) closes 3% of the gap (half in ~23 years) and takes up to 10 a year off the score at full devastation (10 × the state's 0–1 devastation). The national refresh (31 December, and after any event shift or shock) reapplies the two country modifiers, which carry the political capture: `inh_great_fortunes` (multiplier (score − 50)/50 above 50: Aristocrats and Capitalists +20% clout at full) or `inh_dispersed_wealth` ((50 − score)/50 below 50: Aristocrats −20%), reading `te_inh_great_fortunes_mult` / `te_inh_dispersed_mult`. The rest acts per state (below, and § Demographics, Wealth Concentration per state). `te_inh_yearly_update` keeps the old country-level drift (`te_inh_concentration_next`, 3% of the gap) only for a country with no scored state, such as a landless one. Game start: the hidden `inheritance_events.1` (`te_inh_game_start`; on_game_started has no ROOT for the multiplier) seeds every state at its own target, then the national figure, and clears the generation clock history set. A save from before the census starts each state from its owner's figure, or from its own target if the owner has none, at its first yearly pulse, so nothing drifts on load. The score and its target are shown in the law group's description, the concept and the Demographics tab's Wealth section.
- **Old Landowner clout, birth rate and migration** moved off the laws: the clout to the score, birth and migration to the rural modifiers.
- **Rural-scaled modifiers** (state, `te_inh_refresh_rural_effects`, the only refresh site, called by `te_demog_state_yearly` on the state's yearly pulse straight after the census walk): multiplier `te_inh_agrarian_share` = Peasants + Farmers over the state's population, read from the walk's `te_dg_agr_share` (the walk replaced the old `te_inh_agrarian_share_value` pop walk, so the mod walks pops once a year per state; London reads 0.174). Forced Heirship `inh_forced_heirship_rural` (birth −15%, migration quota −20% at full share); Primogeniture `inh_primogeniture_rural` (+10%, +20%); Undivided Farm Succession `inh_undivided_farm_rural` (migration +20%) replaces Forced Heirship's. The quota is symmetric, so scaling by rural share is what makes it a rural-exodus lever.
- **Amendments** (parents from other groups; ways in: debate events `inheritance_events.10`–`.13` in `on_law_checkpoint_debate`, petitions `amendment_petition_events.17`–`.20`). Estate Duties (`amendment_estate_duties`): parent Graduated Taxation, on the four private-estate laws, Political Agitation, not beside the Endowment, no modifier (no standing revenue). Perpetual Dynastic Trusts (`amendment_perpetual_trusts`): parent Laissez-Faire, Freedom of Testation only, Globalization, capitalists' investment pool +10%. Birthright Endowment (`amendment_birthright_endowment`): parent Old Age Pension, the four laws, Universal Basic Income, qualifications +10%, education access +5%, Laborers', Farmers' and Shopkeepers' investment pool +10%; it levies the duty itself, so it replaces Estate Duties. The swap adds the Endowment before removing the duty (`te_inh_swap_duties_for_endowment`), so whether `add_amendment` sees a same-block removal does not matter; `te_inh_tidy_amendments` drops a duty an IG left beside it. Undivided Farm Succession (`amendment_undivided_farm_succession`): parent Peasant Proprietorship, Forced Heirship and Customary, no unlock. The debate events offer to carry an amendment the outgoing law holds over to the incoming one (their `_carry` descriptions).
- **Generation events** (first wave): `.2` The Cadet Sons (Primogeniture), `.3` The Last Division (Forced Heirship, without the farm amendment; one option attaches it), `.4` The Founder's Will (Freedom of Testation), `.5` The Great Estates for Sale (an estate duty held 15 years, `te_inh_duty_years`). Each rolls 10% a year from its own sub-action, has a 25-year cooldown and waits for `te_inh_law_recent`, set for 20 years by every inheritance law's `on_activate`. Options that move the score call `te_inh_shift_concentration` inside a `custom_tooltip`. Not built yet: State as Universal Heir's The Hidden Estates, Possession by Use's The Empty Holding, the Endowment's The First Cohort (spec §5).
- **Posthumous Title** (`.6`, Mind Backups or Biological Immortality, 20% a year, once per country, `te_inh_posthumous_decided`): recognise continuity of title (`te_inh_title_continuity` pins the target; `inh_continuity_of_title`, −10% innovation and qualifications; `.7` The Heirs Who Never Inherit five years later, with a reversal option) or declare legal death (`inh_legal_death_backlash`, Transhumanist movement +25% for ten years).
- **Wealth Concentration's state modifiers** (demographics spec §4.2, phase 2; `te_inh_refresh_wc_state_effects`, the only refresh site, called by `te_demog_state_yearly` straight after the state's drift, and at game start by the hidden state event `inheritance_events.8`, which `.1` fires for every state once they are seeded; ROOT is the state, so the multiplier reads its own score). Each reads the state's `te_dg_wc`: `inh_concentrated_property` ((score − 50)/50 above 50: Aristocrats' and Capitalists' investment pool +10%, radicals +10%, qualifications −10%, education access −0.05 at full), `inh_dispersed_property` ((50 − score)/50 below 50: Farmers' and Shopkeepers' investment pool +10%), `inh_land_hunger` ((score − 50)/50 × the agrarian share `te_dg_agr_share`: migration quota +20%; a third modifier because a multiplier is one variable and the rural exodus needs both factors). Multipliers `te_inh_wc_high_mult`, `te_inh_wc_low_mult`, `te_inh_land_hunger_mult` (state, never removed). A shift or shock made from country scope moves the scores at once and the state modifiers follow at each state's next yearly pulse. All five Wealth Concentration modifiers run under every Demographics rule setting (owner, 2026-10-09).
- **Revolutions**: the score and Posthumous Title flags are variables; `inh_repair_after_civil_war` (called from `te_civil_war_on_won`) copies the loser's over a revolutionary winner's own and reapplies the country modifiers (the state modifiers are the states' and move with them), and `te_inh_apply_concentration_modifiers` restores `inh_continuity_of_title` while its flag holds.
- **Ideology stances** (`ideology_modifications.py`, P C F T S U): traditional SA N D D SD SD, moderate D N N SA SD SD, reform D A A N D D, bonapartist D N SA N D D, progressive (Proletarian) SD N A D A A, communist SD D N SD SA D, anarchist SD D N SD D SA. Hand-written blocks in `extra_ideologies.txt` name all six; Islamic Inheritance D A A D D D; the socialist, imperial-cult and theocratic custom-religion economies approve State as Universal Heir and disapprove Possession by Use.
- **Enactment events** `extra_law_events.17`, `.53`, `.77` fire for Forced Heirship, Freedom of Testation, State as Universal Heir and Possession by Use.

## Rules of War (`lawgroup_rules_of_war`)

Five laws, one identity each (2026-10-09 redesign from a playtest: the old group was one dial at ×1/×2/×3, and the default was a pure malus). Laws in `common/laws/extra_laws.txt` (the RULES OF WAR header), AI trigger `te_row_serious_war` in `common/scripted_triggers/rules_of_war_triggers.txt`, strike bans in `nuclear_program_modifier_types.txt`, pins in `test_rules_of_war.py`. Every country starts on Traditional (`extra_history.txt`).

| Law (key) | Unlock | For | Signature | Other modifiers |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Total War (`law_total_war`) | Chemical Warfare (was Bombing Aircraft) | the nation mobilized | mobilization speed +25%, war-support loss from casualties −50% | kill rate +0.10, devastation +100%, battles ±25% war support, Ministry of War impact +25%, Foreign Affairs cap −1, reputation −10, anti-war +20%, escalation +25% both ways, UN −2; unlocks three PMs |
