| Traditional (`law_traditional_rules_of_war`) | start | the soldier's code | morale loss −5%, army experience +25% | none |
| War Crimes Forbidden (`law_war_crimes_forbidden`) | Wargaming | the law of occupation | radicals from conquest −25% | devastation −25%, kill rate −0.05, Foreign Affairs impact +10%, reputation +2, escalation −10%, UN +0.5 |
| Humanitarian Regulations (`law_humanitarian_regulations`) | Nuclear Weapons | the trusted partner | recovery rate +10%, reputation +6 (the group's highest) | devastation −50%, kill rate −0.05, strategic strike ban, Foreign Affairs impact +25%, anti-war −10%, escalation −10%, UN +1 |
| Limited War (`law_limited_war`) | ICBMs | proportionality, limited aims | war goal maneuver cost +100% in plays it starts, infamy generation −25%, tactical strike ban, escalation −30% (the group's strongest) | battles −20% war support both ways, anti-war −20%, devastation −75%, kill rate −0.05, strategic strike ban, Foreign Affairs impact +25%, reputation +3, UN +1.5 |

- **Limited War prices aims, it doesn't ban them** (owner, 2026-10-09). Gating the subjugation war goals would need `REPLACE:` copies of vanilla war goal types (an `INJECT` can't add to a `possible` block vanilla declares; read for diplomatic actions, `scripting_best_practices.md` § "A second trigger block is rejected at load"), so the law raises `country_initiator_war_goal_maneuver_cost_mult` instead: war goals in plays it starts cost double, still conquerable but fewer at once. A cost increase can't go below zero; Aggressive Coordination's −0.3 (any tier) softens it. Defensive counter-goals are untouched.
- **No law closes a ministry.** Caps move by at most 1 (Ministry of War's law cap is 3, Foreign Affairs' 2). An impact multiplier scales the whole institution block, wage and goods costs included, so it is a mixed lever; only Total War's Ministry of War +25% and the humane laws' Foreign Affairs boosts use one.
- **Nuclear bans are law modifiers**: `country_strategic_nuclear_strikes_forbidden_bool` (Humanitarian Regulations, Limited War) and `country_tactical_nuclear_strikes_forbidden_bool` (Limited War), `script_only` booleans that show in the law tooltip. `nd_war_law_permits_strategic_strike` / `_tactical_strike` read them; `nd_war_law_exception` (struck, a covered country struck, or `nd_fighting_for_existence`) lifts either.
- **AI** (`ai_enact_weight_modifier` only; vanilla's `ai_will_do` is a hard veto): Total War +200 in a serious war (`te_row_serious_war`: at war with a major or great power, or `nd_fighting_for_existence`), −100 at peace; the other four +150 while a country at peace still holds Total War. Enactment time is the default, so a switch costs months, a law slot and the law-change approval swing.
- **Stances** (`ideology_modifications.py`, TW Trad WCF HR LW): aggressive SA A N D SD; traditional N SA N D D; moderate D N A N D; liberal (liberal, liberal_modern, market_liberal, humanitarian) SD D N SA SA; pacifist (pacifist, anarchist) SD D N A SA. The nuclear posture classes compare Total War with Limited War (`nd_stance_*`), so liberals keep Limited War at strongly approve. The six hand-written custom-religion outlooks in `extra_ideologies.txt` name all five.
- **Kept fixed by the redesign:** the UN Authority values (`test_un_policy_pillar.py`), the UN AI hooks (most reward Humanitarian Regulations), the amendments (No-First-Strike Pledge on Humanitarian Regulations / Limited War, Military Necessity Clause on War Crimes Forbidden / Humanitarian Regulations) and the enactment events `extra_law_events.10`, `.11`, `.32`, `.46`, `.47`, `.70`, `.71` (all a humane bill against military pushback).
- **War-crime scandal** `social_tensions_events.7` fires at war under Total War, Traditional or War Crimes Forbidden; its reform option needs Traditional or War Crimes Forbidden (it had needed a humane law the trigger excluded, so it never appeared).

## Demographics (`demographics_rule`): the census (phase 1)

A yearly census of every state's people by single year of age and by sex, shown in a Demographics tab of the Population panel and a Demographics subtab of the state panel. It also keeps Wealth Concentration per state and computes the Gini and the national urban pattern. **Phase 1 applies nothing to population**: births, deaths and the workforce still follow the game's own rules, so the census is a measurement the later phases will act on. Two changes outside population ride with it: Wealth Concentration is now kept per state with four new target terms (§ Wealth Concentration per state), and the tactical nuclear strike counts its soldiers among the dead. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-demographics-design.md` (§ numbers below are its sections); plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-demographics-phases-0-1.md`; engine evidence: `docs/testing/demographics-probe-results-2026-10-08.md`; harness results: `docs/testing/demographics-harness-2026-10-08.md`. The model, harness and spec corrections are PR 1 (#828); the census is PR 2 (branch `demographics-phase1`).

**Not built yet.** Phase 2 (consequences): the retuned defines with the births and deaths modifiers (and the equilibrium that `Display only` and `Disabled` will use), workforce, pension and health bill, conscription, youth bulge, the Family & Reproductive Policy laws and measures, the pension-age setting, §8.4's removals, AI weights, the tab's Family Policy section. Wealth Concentration's effects, split by scope, are built (§ Inheritance, the state modifiers). Phase 3 (place and colour): settlement pattern, the urban pattern's effects and Planned Capital, sex-balance effects, the event wave, biological age, Cultural Hegemony's fertility drift, resettlement profiles, the remaining Wealth Concentration shocks. Map modes stay blocked until a per-state source exists (the `te_map_mode_*` hijack is switched off, `docs/audits/open_issues.md`). Calibration to world history (§2.3's targets) is phase 2's prerequisite.

### Game rule

`demographics_rule`: `demographics_full` (default), `demographics_display_only`, `demographics_disabled`, labelled Full / Display only / Disabled. Every check is written negatively through `te_demog_cohorts_run` (not Disabled) and `te_demog_effects_run` (neither Disabled nor Display only), so a save from before the rule keeps the system. In phase 1 Full and Display only behave the same, since nothing is applied. Disabled runs no cohorts and hides the tab and subtab. It does **not** stop the pop and building walks, Wealth Concentration, the war shock or the national figure: inheritance (#822) owns those and must keep working. No setting gates Wealth Concentration's five modifiers either, the two national ones or the three per state (owner, 2026-10-09). Keys: `rule_demographics_rule`, `setting_demographics_*` (and `_desc`) in `te_game_rules_l_english.yml`.

### Files

| Area | Files |

| Model and tooling | `scripts/analysis/demographics_model.py` (the reference), `demographics_params.py` (every rate, weight and table), `pop_growth.py` (the engine's growth curves from `extra_defines.txt`), `demographics_save_inputs.py` (plain-text save reader), `demographics_harness.py` (CLI), `demographics_observer_report.py` (census-log summary); `test_demographics_model.py`, `_harness.py`, `_save_inputs.py`, `_observer_report.py`, `test_pop_growth.py`, fixture `test_fixtures/demographics/gb_1836_slice.v3` |
| Generator | `scripts/generators/gen_demographics.py` writes `te_demog_generated_effects.txt` and `te_demog_generated_values.txt`; `test_gen_demographics.py` fails CI when they are stale |
| Script | `te_demog_effects.txt` (walks, seed, step, flows, figures, country census), `te_demog_wealth_effects.txt` (Gini, Wealth Concentration), `te_demog_history_effects.txt` (yearly samples), `te_demog_values.txt`, `te_demog_display_values.txt` (trend codes, pyramid ranges), `te_demog_triggers.txt`, `te_demog_sguis.txt` (read-only display gates), `te_demog_on_actions.txt`, `events/te_demog_events.txt`, `te_demog_modifiers.txt`, `demographics_modifier_types.txt`; `test_demographics_registry.py` (static wiring plus a small interpreter that runs the script's effects against the model) |
| GUI | `gui/pops_overview.gui` (full override of the Population panel with a fifth tab), `gui/te_demographics_widgets.gui` (every section as a type), `gui/states_panel.gui` (fourth Population subtab), `te_system_tab_sguis.txt` (tab gates); `docs/guides/gui_modding_guide.md` lists the overrides |
| Test console | `events/te_debug_demog_events.txt`, `te_debug_demog_effects.txt`, `te_debug_demog_values.txt`, `te_debug_demog_modifiers.txt` |

### When it runs

| Pulse | Scope | Runs |
|---|---|---|
| `on_game_started` | hidden country event `te_demog_events.1` per country | `te_demog_country_game_start`: walk every state and, if the cohorts run, seed it (marked `te_dg_reseed`, since the seed is off the state's pulse), then the country's figures. Wealth Concentration's own seed is `te_inh_game_start` (`inheritance_events.1`), which then fires the hidden state event `inheritance_events.8` in every state for its three state modifiers |
| `on_yearly_pulse_state` (a different day per state across the year) | state | `te_demog_state_yearly`: walks, then inheritance's rural modifiers, then Wealth Concentration's drift and its state modifiers (`te_inh_refresh_wc_state_effects`), then (cohorts run) seed, skip or step. No census yet, a census more than a year old, an emptied ring, people more than 25% above or below the last census's (a state merged or split), or a seed made off this pulse (`te_dg_reseed`: game start, the console's re-seeds): seed. A census exactly one year old: step. A second pulse in one year does nothing |
| `on_monthly_pulse_country` | country | `te_demog_country_monthly`: snapshot the owner's war dead (a rise only) into the year's total, whatever the rule; while the cohorts run, share the month's rise to the states at once (`te_demog_share_war_dead`) |
| `on_yearly_pulse_country` (31 December) | country | `te_demog_country_yearly`: the war shock, then the year's war dead reset; the national Wealth Concentration, Gini and #822's modifiers (`te_demog_wc_national`); then (cohorts run) `te_demog_country_census`: sums, lists, urban pattern, trend flags, the player's outline, the history sample |
| `on_lost_war` | country on the losing side | `te_demog_wc_shock = { AMOUNT = -5 }` when the war cost it a war goal (`exists = scope:victim_of_wargoal`), whatever the rule |

`te_inheritance_on_actions.txt` no longer has its own `on_yearly_pulse_state` hook. Two more hooks sit in other files: the known-kill sites (below) and the banking crash.

### The census

- **Ring.** Each census state keeps `te_dg_f<k>` and `te_dg_m<k>` for k = 0–149, the birth year mod 150 (150 one-year slots, women and men apart), plus a pool (`te_dg_pf`, `te_dg_pm`, mean age `te_dg_pa`) for ages 150 and over. A slot nobody holds has no variable, and the step skips it. **People, not shares**: values are stored in units of 1e-5, and a one-year cohort of 90-year-olds is a share near 1e-5, so its deaths would round away. `te_dg_scale` ties the ring to the engine's population and is applied lazily at the next sweep, while the display bands and class sums are scaled at once.
- **Seed.** The state's own life table times a growth factor read from a table by net reproduction rate gives the stable structure of its rates (§2.6), scaled to `state_population`; the seed's raw ring (radix 100,000) is normalised to people in two multiplications (see `scripting_best_practices.md`, § Writing a Yearly Per-Age Model in Script). Every 1836 state is seeded at game start and seeded again at its first pulse, so its first step, a year after that, compares a year's population change with a year's natural change (a step on a census taken off the pulse would compare 12 to 24 months with one year, and read the difference as migrants). A state without a census, or with one more than a year old, is seeded at its pulse.
- **Step** (once a year per state): `te_demog_step_begin` (the year's rates into locals, then the flows) and `te_demog_step_run` (the sweep over the ring, then the year's totals and the scale). The sweep is two guarded passes in age order (generated, `te_demog_sweep`); each age takes its group's death probability from five causes (infection and hunger, work, accidents and violence, maternal, chronic and old age), each a base schedule times a multiplier from literacy, standard of living, technology, laws and institutions; the newborn enter at 0 from fertility (§2.3: wealth, then education, child survival and urban life lower the desired number, and the means to plan a family close the gap). Flows are applied by **rate age** (last year's age), so the war window 18–40 and the migrant classes use that age.
- **Flows** (§2.5). War dead: the monthly pulse sums each war's `num_country_dead` for the owner, keeps the positive change, and shares it at once among the states by soldiers; a state's step takes what gathered since its last step (`te_dg_war_in`), 95% men aged 18–40, so the dead cover the same months as its population change (shared on 31 December, a state stepping in October would read March's battle dead as emigrants, then as immigrants the next year). Known kills: nuclear strikes and Violent Hostility record their dead on the state (`te_demog_note_kills`); the tactical strike now tallies the soldiers' half too, so the figure `nuclear_weapon_events.5`/`.6` print rises. Migration is the residual: the population change less the engine's own natural change (estimated from the pop walk), war dead and kills; inside 0.3% of the population it is model error and becomes 0; a move over 25% in a year re-seeds the state instead (a merge or a split, not migration); otherwise it is shared over migrant classes by a profile built from the destination's jobs, transport, women's rights and the origin's crisis (`te_demog_crisis`: devastation, turmoil and war).
- **Figures.** Each state stores bands (5 years, 0–4 to 85+; `te_dg_bf<b>`, `te_dg_bm<b>`), median age, age shares, dependency ratio, sex balance, children per woman, life expectancy at birth and 65, infant mortality, births and deaths per 1,000, and net migration. The country's census sums the bands, weights the rates (children per woman by women aged 15–49, life expectancy by people), counts urban people (everyone outside a rural workplace, Peasants counting as rural) and sets primacy, the effective number of cities and the pattern (Primate 40% or more, Dominant 25%, Balanced 10%, Dispersed). The player's country alone also gets the outline (`te_dg_pjf<b>`, `te_dg_pjm<b>`): its bands carried twenty years ahead, five years at a time, on its own average rates with no migration.
- **Trend arrows.** Each state and country keeps `te_dg_prev_<name>` from a step; `te_dg_stepped` is 1 when the figures came from a step and `te_dg_trend_ok` is 1 only when this census and the one before were steps. A seed writes no births or deaths, so the first step's arrows would compare with nothing: arrows start at the second step (in an 1836 start, the 31 December 1838 country census). The panel draws an arrow only while `te_dg_trend_ok` is 1 and hides births and deaths per 1,000 while `te_dg_stepped` is 0.
- **Lists** on the country (variable lists the panel draws, `te_demog_country_lists`): `te_dg_states` (its census states, largest first by the people their own census counted, the figure each row shows, at most 40), `te_dg_cities` (its three census states of the largest cities by `state_city_size_rank`). The number of effective cities is the scalar `te_dg_n_cities`; the list keeps `te_dg_cities` because the engine may keep a variable and a list in one namespace. A revolution's winner inherits `te_dg_census_year` but no list, so the civil-war repair (`inh_repair_after_civil_war`) draws them again at once.
- **History.** `te_demog_history_record` keeps a yearly sample (median age, children per woman, life expectancy, Gini, Wealth Concentration) in a container per year on `te_dg_hist` for countries `te_history_country_is_tracked` accepts (the player and major powers), at most 100 years. The second 1836 census overwrites the game-start sample.

### Variable families

All census variables start `te_dg_` (short: there are about 345 on a state, and each name is in the save); effects, values and loc start `te_demog_`. The full inventory is the header of `te_demog_effects.txt`. In brief:

| Family | Scope | Variables |
|---|---|---|
| Walk (every year, whatever the rule) | state | `te_dg_walk_pop`, `_lit`, `_sol`, `_wtfr`, `_eb`, `_ed`, `_agr_share`, `_soldiers`, `_bureaucrats`, `_urban`, `_urban_share`, `_fjob_share`, strata counts `_n_lo/_mi/_up` and incomes `_y_lo/_mi/_up`, `_gini`, building levels by holder `_lv_priv/_self/_ctry/_own` |
| Ring and scale | state | `te_dg_f<k>`, `_m<k>`, `_pf`, `_pm`, `_pa`, `_scale`, `_raw`, `_people`, `_pop_last`, `_year` |
| Bands and classes | state | `te_dg_bf<b>`, `_bm<b>`; migrant classes `_cf<c>`, `_cm<c>`; the 18–40 sums `_f1840`, `_m1840` |
| Rates and figures | state | `te_dg_e0`, `_e65`, `_imr`, `_tfr`, `_means`, `_desired`, the cause multipliers `_m_inf/_ext/_chr/_work`, `_median`, shares, `_dependency`, `_sex_balance`, `_cbr`, `_cdr`, with `te_dg_prev_<name>`; `te_dg_pyr_max` (the pyramid's scale, stored at the census so the bars don't each read 36 bands a frame; a country's too) |
| Flows | state / country | state: `te_dg_war_in`, `_kills_in`, `_net_migration`, `_inflow_years`, `te_dg_reseed` (a seed off the pulse, made again at the next); country: `te_dg_war_seen`, `_war_year`, `_war_shock` |
| Country census | country | `te_dg_census_year`, `te_dg_cbf<b>`, `_cbm<b>`, the weighted figures above, `_urban_share`, `_primacy`, `_n_cities`, `_urban_label`, the outline `_pjf<b>`, `_pjm<b>`, lists `te_dg_states`, `_cities`, the history list `te_dg_hist` |
| Trend | state / country | `te_dg_stepped`, `te_dg_trend_ok`, `te_dg_prev_*` |
| Wealth Concentration | state / country | state: `te_dg_wc`, `_wc_target`, the state modifiers' multipliers `te_inh_wc_high_mult`, `te_inh_wc_low_mult`, `te_inh_land_hunger_mult`; country: `te_inh_concentration` (the national figure), `te_dg_wc_target`, the target's terms `te_dg_wc_t_law/_land/_own/_ineq/_tax`, `te_dg_wc_top`, `_bottom`, `te_dg_war_shock` |

### Wealth Concentration per state

Spec §4.2. A state's target is `owner.te_inh_law_amendment_target` (the inheritance law and amendments, #822) plus four terms (`te_demog_values.txt`, `te_demog_generated_values.txt`), clamped 0–100:

- **Land tenure**: Serfdom and Manorialism +15, Tenant Farmers and the two Latifundias +5, Commercialized Agriculture 0, Peasant Proprietorship and Homesteading −10, Collectivized Agriculture −20, times the state's agrarian share. The four variants take their parent's value (the generator expands them from the laws' `parent` fields).
- **Ownership**: 40 × (private share of the state's capital levels − 0.9), capped ±20. **Centred on 0.9, the 1836 reference**, so the term is near 0 at the start and falls as cooperative and state property grow (+4 at most, when everything is private). The plan's centre of 0.5 put every country near +20 from day one and switched Great Family Fortunes on where #822 opened at 50; calibration sets the centre. Capital is the walk's private, cooperative and state levels; the ownership buildings (Manor Houses, Financial Districts, company headquarters) read self-owned and are left out, as Subsistence Farms (read private) and government buildings (read state-owned) are.
- **Income inequality**: 50 × (the state's Gini − 0.4), capped ±15. The 1836 Ginis are 0.40–0.46 for most countries (Britain 0.52), so the term is 0 to +3 there, +6 for Britain.
- **Taxes on wealth** (country): Graduated Taxation −5; under the tax code, 20 × the enacted dividend rate (`te_tax_view_en_div_rate`) more; at most −10.

Continuity of title pins the target at the score. Each state drifts 3% of the gap a year and loses up to 10 a year at full devastation (10 × its 0–1 devastation). The national figure is the states' average weighted by where property is held: each level of the ownership buildings and of self-owned buildings counts 1, and the country's state-owned levels are shared by the state's share of the country's bureaucrats × 0.1 (when the weights total under 1 the states weigh by people, and by 1 each if people total under 1 too). It is the one refresh site of `te_inh_concentration` and the two country modifiers (`te_demog_wc_national`), which also rewrites each scored state's target from the law and taxes as they stand, sets the term bars for the panel and the most and least concentrated state (each shown only while that state exists and is still the country's). The target the law tooltip, the concept and the panel show (`te_inh_concentration_target`) moves at once with a law, amendment or tax change: the stored target shifts by the change in the law and tax terms since the refresh, which move every state alike, and the panel's law and tax bars read those terms live. Under continuity of title a note under the bars says why they no longer add up to the target. Shocks (`te_demog_wc_jump` moves every scored state, `te_demog_wc_shock` also refreshes the national figure; a country with no scored state moves its own figure): a year at war −1 to every state, at most −10 until peace (`te_demog_wc_war_shock`, first in the 31 December pulse, once the year's war dead pass 0.2% of the population); a lost war that cost it a war goal −5 (`scope:victim_of_wargoal`: a co-belligerent that gave up nothing is spared); a winning revolution of the left (Council Republic or Trade Unions in government) −20, from `inh_repair_after_civil_war` reading `var:te_cw_rebels_won`; a banking crash −5 at both `banking_crisis_wave_crashed` sites. The upward and event-driven shocks (§4.2) are phase 3.

**What it moves** (§4.2's table, phase 2). The national figure sets political strength through two country modifiers, `inh_great_fortunes` and `inh_dispersed_wealth`; each state's own score sets the rest in that state through `inh_concentrated_property`, `inh_dispersed_property` and, in farming states, `inh_land_hunger` (values and refresh in § Inheritance). A state well above its country's figure (a capital full of Financial Districts, a serf province) carries the costs of concentration while the country as a whole does not.

The Gini is the grouped Gini over the three strata (`te_demog_gini_from_locals`, one formula for a state and the country, a 3-comparator sorting network), `0.3 + 0.85 × grouped`, so Britain 1836 reads 0.52 (§4.1; the scale is set by the harness's `gini` command).

### Family Limitation

`country_fertility_means_add` (`demographics_modifier_types.txt`, script-only) is the one way to shift the means to plan a family: history, events and phase 2's measures grant it. `te_demog_family_limitation` carries +0.6 (`FAMILY_LIMITATION_MEANS`), and `extra_history.txt` gives it to France, whose fertility transition began about 1800 (§2.6). That is the only line in the system that names a country. Phase 2 turns the means shifts of laws and measures (`MEANS_LAW_SHIFT`) into this modifier type.

### Generator and harness

- `python3 scripts/generators/gen_demographics.py` (`--check` exits 1 when stale) writes the ring's sweeps, the age-group rate tables, the engine's growth curves as per-pop values, the cause multipliers, the migrant profiles, the normalisation effects and the debug replay lines from `demographics_params.py`, `extra_defines.txt` (including `MAX_INSTITUTION_INVESTMENT`), `00_buy_packages.txt` and the land-reform laws. It is not a post-load regenerator: run it after changing any input, and commit both files with the change. `docs/auto_generated_files.md` has the row.
- `python3 scripts/analysis/demographics_harness.py` has `sketch` (the equilibrium of §2.2/§2.3's cases), `inputs`, `gini`, `seed` and `natural-change` and `replay` (below); `docs/guides/python_tools.md` has the full list. Everything numeric lives in `demographics_params.py`: change it, regenerate, and `test_gen_demographics.py` and `test_demographics_registry.py` (which interprets the script against the model) check that script and model still agree.
- `event te_debug_demog.1` (game console; `events/te_debug_demog_events.txt`): **a** replay one year of the capital's census to `debug.log` for `demographics_harness.py replay`; **b** census log on (`TE_DEMOG_CENSUS:` lines each 31 December; **b_off** turns it off) for `demographics_observer_report.py`; **c** re-seed every state; **d** benchmark the step; **e** war-dead accounting; **f**/**g** the working-adult skew probe (`te_demog_debug_working_adults`). Details are in `docs/guides/python_tools.md`.

### Owner checks in game

1. Natural change: two plain-text saves a month apart in a new 1836 game, `demographics_harness.py natural-change OLD NEW`; the ratio should be near 1 before modifiers.
2. Replay: `event te_debug_demog.1` option a, then `demographics_harness.py replay debug.log` prints `OK` for the capital (worst slot error under 1%).
3. The 1836 census: the tab and subtab show a pyramid on day one; Britain's median about 20, children per woman 5.5–6, life expectancy about 40; China's lower. This is also the check that the seed's large products (`state_population × 100000` in the normalisation, about 2.5×10^11 for a large state) evaluate in the engine, as vanilla's products of that size do: a garbled pyramid or a population far off the state's means they don't.
4. An observer run to 2100 with the census log on: fertility falls with standard of living, literacy and the Pill; life expectancy rises with medicine; Gini about 0.5 in 1836 Britain. This is phase 1's gate (§12).
5. Benchmark (option d) in a late-game save: the yearly cost stays near the 1836 figure per occupied slot.
6. War dead (option e) during a war, and whether battle dead come out of home-state pops (§14 Q4).
7. Skew (options f, g): how fast a working-adult modifier changes a pop's workforce share (§14 Q11).
8. The bareword reads: the census line's `crisis` moves with `devastation` and `turmoil`. The census reads a state's devastation as a 0–1 share, as vanilla's trigger comparisons do (`devastation >= 0.2`, `> 0.1`, `>= 0.5`; `add_devastation` takes points): the check confirms that `crisis` rises by the `devastation` the line prints, and by its `turmoil` (0–1) too.
9. Wealth Concentration: #822's law tooltip and the concept still show the score and its target; the inheritance events still move it; a banking crash lowers it; a few countries' 1836 figures against the law targets.
10. Save size: a 1900 save with the census against one with the rule Disabled (the owner's §13 cohort-width call; about 26 MB of an 1836 plain-text save as built).

### Compatibility (§11.5)

The Demography mod models ages too, so running both applies two sets of corrections (no detection; the rule is the way out) and it replaces `states_panel.gui`. The Population panel is a new full-file override (`pops_overview.gui`), so any other mod that replaces it collides with this one. The state panel already collided with Demography; the subtab adds nothing new.

## Penal Labor Camps (`law_penal_labor_camps`)

The authoritarian end of `lawgroup_criminal_justice` (`common/laws/extra_laws.txt`), beside Punishment-Focused (progressiveness 0, the default), Restorative (25, `behaviorism`) and Rehabilitation-Focused (50, `mental_health_awareness`).

- **Gate:** `mass_surveillance` plus one of `law_autocracy` / `law_bakufu` / `law_neo_absolutism` / `law_single_party_state` / `law_outlawed_dissent`. The two Autocracy variants are listed because `unlocking_laws` matches exact keys. A country that loses every gate is moved to Punishment-Focused by the generated `te_fix_inconsistent_lawgroup_criminal_justice` (closest progressiveness; the law is -50). The sweep runs from `on_law_activated`, so when the replacing Distribution of Power or Free Speech law activates, and on the monthly country pulse. `cultural_hegemony.16` never presses the camps on a country: its `activate_law` would skip the gate.
- **Modifier:** +100 authority, -10 legitimacy, -0.10 suppression attraction factor (Shinsengumi's value; vanilla multiplies a suppressed movement's attraction by -0.5 plus the factor, so under Outlawed Dissent, itself -0.5, suppression goes from -1.0 to -1.1 × base), +10% civil-rights movement support, and a flat +5% throughput for mines, logging camps, plantations and rubber plantations. The throughput is deliberately flat (owner call): nothing scales it with radicals, turmoil or prisoners. There is no activism cut: repression feeds the opposition as a cost, so the movement levers are the civil-rights support and the movement stances below.
- **Interest groups:** by ideology stance only (`ideology_modifications.py`; the six custom-religion society ideologies in `extra_ideologies.txt`). Liberal, progressive and moderate criminal-justice ideologies strongly disapprove (so the Intelligentsia and Trade Unions); regressive ones approve, one step below their Punishment-Focused stance, so they don't push for camps. The ideologies that rank the camps above Punishment-Focused, and so push for them, are the fascist leader and movement (strongly approve) and the authoritarian leader (approve); none has a Punishment-Focused stance. The totalitarian society strongly approves both laws, and the imperial-cult and theocratic societies approve both, so they are indifferent between them. The liberal, radical and anarchist movement ideologies strongly disapprove, which is how the law feeds those movements. `test_penal_labor_law.py` pins that every ideology with a Criminal Justice stance has one on the camps.
- **Where else it is listed:** the UN human-rights violator test (`un_regime_human_rights_violator`) and the human-rights vote lean, refusal and withdrawal weights beside Outlawed Dissent; the Mental Health JE's fail condition and the punitive AI weights beside Punishment-Focused (`mental_health_events.3`, `social_tensions_events.5`).
- **Not listed** in the reform-debate enactment events (`extra_law_events.12/.30/.48/.72`) or either Criminal Justice amendment, which are written for the reform laws and Punishment-Focused.

## On-Actions Reference

The mod uses 42 on-action files under `common/on_actions/`; the index below lists the main ones. These wire mod logic into engine hooks — either **pulse-based** (fires periodically for all relevant scopes) or **immediate** (fires the instant a specific game event occurs).

### File Index

| File | Purpose | Hook Type |
|------|---------|-----------|
| `extra_on_actions.txt` | Central hub: pulse wiring, dynamic modifiers, immediate triggers, company cleanup | Mixed |
| `te_construction_market_on_actions.txt` | Construction-market site re-placement on diplomatic/territorial and building-lifecycle changes, plus its yearly heartbeat | Mixed |
| `headlines.txt` | "World first" tech notifications (`on_acquired_technology`) | Immediate |
| `law_events_on_actions.txt` | Law enactment checkpoint events (advance/debate/stall) | Immediate |
| `amendment_on_actions.txt` | Temporary-amendment expiry follow-up events (`on_amendment_timeout`) | Immediate |
| `amendment_petition_on_actions.txt` | Amendment petitions on laws in force, one yearly roll per petition (§ Amendment Petitions) | Pulse |
| `langreform_events_on_actions.txt` | Language reform yearly random events | Pulse |
| `minor_events_on_actions.txt` | Miscellaneous yearly random events | Pulse |
| `repeatable_events_on_actions.txt` | Generic repeatable yearly events | Pulse |
| `resettlement_on_actions.txt` | Internal resettlement: the monthly country pulse (transfer, politics, Declaration, events) and the monthly state pulse (modifier refresh: destination bonuses and readouts) | Pulse (monthly) |
| `social_tensions_on_actions.txt` | Social/political tension yearly events | Pulse |
| `space_race_on_actions.txt` | Space race in-progress and cross-system yearly events | Pulse |
| `te_monetary_on_actions.txt` | Monetary policy: world reference rate and (phase 3) the world rate, the monthly country update, the yearly growth snapshot, the country-creation hooks (see **Monetary Policy (phase 1)**), and — phase 5 — the anchored-GDP refresh on the global pulse, the yearly arrangement scan + seigniorage re-sum, `on_country_default` (the guarantee is called) and `on_become_subject` (currency boards) | Mixed |
| `te_region_area_on_actions.txt` | Writes each state region's land area once per save (`te_region_area_generated.txt`) | Mixed |
| `un_on_actions.txt` | UN formation and recurring member events | Pulse (monthly) |
| `wonder_events_on_actions.txt` | Wonder building narrative events | Pulse (monthly) |
| `te_tax_on_actions.txt` | Legislated tax code (rule-gated): the global monthly dispatch of the processor `te_tax.1` on the 1st, the country-pulse watchdog, migration at game start and for formed and released countries, the carrier re-assert on `on_law_activated`, relief marks on state transfers | Mixed |
| `te_civil_war_on_actions.txt` | The shared civil-war layer: outbreak, win and resolve hooks, which call each system's copy and repair (the tax code's `te_tax_on_uprising` and `te_tax_repair_after_civil_war` among them) | Immediate |

### Pulse Wiring (`extra_on_actions.txt`)

All pulse-based on_actions are routed through `extra_on_actions.txt`:

**`on_monthly_pulse_state`** (Root = State):
- `space_elevator_on_action` — wonder construction progress
- `solar_collector_on_action` — wonder construction progress
- `orbital_battlestation_on_action` — wonder construction progress
- `mind_upload_nexus_on_action` — wonder construction progress
- `antimatter_facility_on_action` — wonder construction progress
- `pollution_on_action` — state pollution modifier update
- `war_propaganda_on_action` — wartime propaganda effects
- `state_yearly_cultural_acceptance_add_on_action` — cultural acceptance
- `tourism_on_action` — tourism output/throughput modifier refresh (sole owner; the multipliers read monthly-varying `city_size_rank` and live building levels)
- `ministry_of_thought_control_on_action` — monthly loyalists from `state_monthly_loyalists_add`, per incorporated state

**`on_monthly_pulse`** (Root = global):
- `city_rank_on_action` — city tier updates
- `global_warming_events_on_action` — GW threshold & recurring events

**`on_monthly_pulse_country`** (Root = Country):
- `remove_invalid_buildings` — space program placement validation
- `cheaty_on_action` — debug modifier (disabled)
- `combined_arms_update_on_action` — military doctrine bonuses
- `investment_pool_setup_on_action` — investment pool management
- `international_relations_events_on_action` — diplo events
- `decolonization_events_on_action` — decolonization narrative
- `movement_events_te_on_action` — political movement events
- `society_technology_events_on_action` — society tech events
- `world_war_events_on_action` — world war events
- `overbuild_protection_on_action` — prevents wonder overbuilding
- `aptitude_traits_cleanup_on_action` — strips aptitude traits from characters the game rules don't allow them on

**`on_yearly_pulse_state`** (Root = State):
- `global_warming_update_on_action` — GHG emissions calculation
- `te_homeland_monthly` — dynamic homeland progress (monthly state pulse)
- `violent_hostility_on_action` — cultural violence
- `migration_crowding_on_action` — migration pull reduction
- `religious_mission_conversion_on_action` — treaty-based conversion

**`on_yearly_pulse_country`** (Root = Country):
- `tech_spread_on_action` — technology diffusion
- `excess_private_construction_on_action` — Excess Private Construction (per-project construction cap raise with an efficiency penalty, removed the year it is no longer needed) and Overinvestment
- `fix_incompatible_laws` — auto-fix illegal law combos
- `construction_cost_scaling_on_action` — GDP-based construction costs
- `colonial_collapse_on_action` — tiny, poor AI countries absorbed or decentralized
- `assign_aptitude_traits_on_action` — character trait assignment

### Immediate Triggers (`extra_on_actions.txt`)

These fire instantly when the engine event occurs, providing same-tick responsiveness:

**`on_company_disbanded`** (Root = Country, scope:company = disbanded company):
- Calls `remove_disbanded_company_buildings_effect` on every state to remove buildings belonging to the disbanded company. Uses a 64-entry if/else_if mapping in `common/scripted_effects/company_building_cleanup_effects.txt`.

**`on_building_built`** (Root = Building):
- When a barracks is built, recalculates `apply_combined_arms_bonuses` for the owning country (new combat unit type enters a formation). Only fires if `country_combined_arms_bonus_enabled_bool` is true.
- Also used by the construction market (`te_construction_market_on_actions.txt`) to remove a finished state's construction site.
- Grand Monuments: `monument_events.1` (vanity backlash, the dedication ceremony, a refresh). It missed a monument whose five queued levels finished on the same day, so the ceremony also has a check in the monthly refresh (§ Grand Monuments).

**`on_character_recruitment`** (Root = Character):
- Calls `update_characters` on the owner to immediately apply tech-based traits (immortality, logistics) to newly recruited characters instead of waiting for monthly pulse.
- Recalculates `apply_combined_arms_bonuses` for the owner (new general may change formation composition).

**`on_military_formation_created`** (Root = Military Formation):
- Recalculates `apply_combined_arms_bonuses` for the formation's country. Uses `country` accessor (not `owner`) to reach country scope from formation scope.

**`on_state_owner_change`** (Root = state):
- Removes `building_space_program` if the state has one and is not the new owner's market capital. Catches conquest scenarios same-tick instead of waiting for monthly `remove_invalid_buildings`.

**`on_acquired_technology`** (Root = Country):
- `add_arable_land_effect_on_action` — arable land from agricultural techs
- `fix_incompatible_laws` — law compatibility check
- `cultural_hegemony_tech_first_on_action` — world-first tech prestige (`cultural_hegemony_on_actions.txt`)
- `agricultural_diffusion_on_action` — broadcasts the first researcher of each diffusion-eligible tech

**`on_country_formed`** (Root = the new country), **`on_country_released_as_{independent,own_subject,overlord_subject,company_subject}`** (scope:target = the released country):
- `agdiff_backfill_on_country_formed` / `agdiff_backfill_on_released_country` — give the new country every agricultural-diffusion modifier whose world-first has already fired (`agdiff_backfill_diffusion_for_country`, stateless). The broadcast only reaches countries that exist at the time. Unlike the broadcast (recognized countries only), this backfill ignores country type; that predates #460 and is left as it was.

**Civil wars** (the shared hooks in `te_civil_war_on_actions.txt`, #467). `te_civil_war_on_won` runs `te_civil_war_resolve_sides`, then nuclear custody and the #460–#465 repairs (`agdiff_repair_after_civil_war`, `un_repair_after_civil_war`, `te_monetary_repair_after_civil_war`, `cr_repair_after_civil_war`, `sr_repair_after_civil_war`), then `te_civil_war_clear`. The agricultural-diffusion ones:
- `agdiff_on_uprising`, from `te_civil_war_on_start` at `on_revolution_start` / `on_secession_start` (Root = the original, scope:target = the uprising country) — gives the rebels each diffusion modifier the original holds (`agdiff_copy_diffusion_from_root`), and nothing the nation lacked, and marks them with `agdiff_cw_copied`. The rebel object is the one that survives a rebel victory, and a revolution's winner inherits no modifier from the loser.
- `agdiff_repair_after_civil_war`, from `te_civil_war_on_won` at `on_civil_war_won` (Root = the winner) — the backfill again, only when the revolutionaries won a revolution (`var:te_cw_rebels_won = 1`), the winner carries no `agdiff_cw_copied` (a backstop for wars begun before the rebels got the copy), and it is recognized; the marker is removed here. On a rebel win only (`te_cw_rebels_won = 1`), it also restores what is left of a lost `agdiff_first_mover_prestige`, read from `agdiff_first_mover_month` (recorded with the title) off a ladder of whole years left, and not restored for titles won before that variable existed, and re-points `first_<tech>_country` from the dead loser to the winner.

**`on_law_activated`** (Root = Law scope):
- `fix_incompatible_laws_from_law_scope` — law compatibility
- `te_fix_inconsistent_laws_from_law_scope` — generated per-lawgroup consistency cleanup
- `language_reform_law_on_action` — language reform init/cleanup
- `radical_law_backlash_on_action` — backlash events for radical laws
- `banking_law_cascade_on_law_activated` — command economy force-enacts state-owned banking

**`on_law_enactment_started`** (Root = Law scope):
- Fires `minor_events_timelineextended.2` (law enactment notification)

**`on_amendment_timeout`** (Root = Country, scope:amendment = expired amendment, scope:law = its law):
- `te_amendment_timeout_on_action` — routes to the sunset-clause expiry events (`amendment_on_actions.txt`; see § Temporary Amendments)

**`on_merge_markets`** (Root = dissolving market, scope:market = absorbing market):
- `gw_market_join_on_action` removes the dissolving leader's fossil-fuel tariff-rate modifier without writing native tariff levels. National climate policies remain with the country. Monthly `gw_refresh_tariff_policies_effect` clears lost customs authority; it never synchronizes national policies.


### Construction-Market Immediate Triggers (`te_construction_market_on_actions.txt`)

The construction market hooks many engine events to keep its construction sites placed on the current map:
- `on_merge_markets`, `on_create_market` — market changes
- `on_country_released_as_independent/own_subject/overlord_subject` — country releases
- `on_country_formed`, `on_capitulation`, `on_enemy/ally_capitulated_notification` — country events
- `on_diplo_play_back_down`, `on_war_end` — diplomatic resolutions
- `on_revolution_start`, `on_secession_start` — internal conflicts
- `on_wargoal_enforced` — war results
- `on_start_expanding_building`, `on_building_expanded`, `on_building_built` — building events
- `on_production_method_changed` — removes `pm_retooling` in games whose rule setting waives it (§ Market settings without retooling or maintenance)

### Scope Chain Limitation (Important)

Building, institution and law scopes **do not support variables or modifiers**. The state modifier refresh effects in `extra_effects.txt` use `add_modifier = { multiplier = script_value }`, which evaluates the script value through the parent scope chain, so calling them from building-scope hooks like `on_building_built` / `on_production_method_changed`, from `on_law_activated`, or from `on_acquired_technology` causes cascading errors. Those hooks have all been removed (a plain `remove_modifier` on the building itself is fine: `te_remove_waived_pm_retooling` runs from `on_start_expanding_building` and `on_production_method_changed`) — the **periodic state pulses are the only refresh sites**: `pollution_on_action` and `tourism_on_action` on `on_monthly_pulse_state`, `migration_crowding_on_action` and `free_port_tariff_update_on_action` on `on_yearly_pulse_state`. Pick the pulse whose cadence matches how fast the multiplier's inputs move, and give each modifier exactly one refresh site.

## AI Weights Guidelines

- **Journal entries:** `weight` determines priority in the JE list. Global issues use 1000+, personal/national issues use 10.
- **Scripted buttons:** `ai_chance = { value = 0 ... }` — base value with conditional `add` modifiers.
- **Diplomatic actions:** Key fields: `evaluation_chance`, `will_propose`, `propose_score`, `will_break`.
- **Treaty articles:** Key fields: `article_ai_usage = { offer request none }`, `evaluation_chance`, `inherent_accept_score`.
- Always look at **vanilla files** for AI pattern reference.

## Company AI

The engine picks one company at a time for the AI to work toward: among the types whose `ai_will_do` holds (and at least `attainable`), weighted by `ai_weight` (engine default 1) times its `NAI` `COMPANY_TYPE_*` factors (stickiness ×3 for 60 months, a formable bonus, predicted productivity, competition with companies already held). While planning it builds toward the type's `ai_construction_targets`. Numbers live in `common/script_values/te_company_ai_values.txt`.

- **Weights.** Vanilla gives its flavored companies 3 and generic ones nothing (1). The mod's 82 flavored companies get `te_company_ai_weight_flavored` (10), plus `te_company_ai_weight_home` (10) while the country holds one of the company's `preferred_headquarters` states; its 11 new generic companies get `te_company_ai_weight_generic` (3). `REPLACE:`/`INJECT:` entries are vanilla's companies and keep vanilla's weight.
- **Construction targets need a `state_trigger`.** Vanilla's `companies.md`: a target with an empty state trigger does nothing. Every mod target now names the company's preferred HQ states, the capital, or a state that already has the building (that last one covers mines and rigs whose resource the capital lacks). `test_company_ai.py` fails on a target without one, a mod flavored company without `ai_weight`, or a buildable flagship without `ai_value`.
- **Flagships** (`common/buildings/company_buildings.txt`) carry `ai_value = te_company_flagship_ai_value` (50000, vanilla's top tier). Their only max level comes from the company's prosperity bonus (on at 100, off below 75), so only a prosperous company's flagship can be built at all. Before the `ai_value`, the AI built 9% (2029 observer save) to 14% (2073 save) of its prosperous companies' flagships. If `ai_value` doesn't move that, the fallback is a decision that places the flagship (the Strategic Reserve hub and Space Program precedent).
- **Failing companies.** The engine's AI never disbands a company by choice (observer run 1972–2029: no surviving country dropped one company for another), so its slots fill before the mod's late-era companies unlock. `te_company_failing_monthly_effect` (`te_company_effects.txt`, on `on_monthly_pulse_country`) counts each company's consecutive monthly checks that fail either test (`te_company_fails_this_month`): prosperity below `te_company_failing_prosperity` (10), or owned levels (`company_owned_levels`) below `te_company_small_share` (0.1%) of the country's `building_levels`, saved as `scope:te_company_min_levels` before the iteration. The count lives in the company variable `te_company_months_failing`; an AI country removes one company a check once a count reaches `te_company_failing_months` (60, about 4.9 years), then runs the flagship cleanup. The size test catches a prosperous company too small to matter to a large economy; since the engine dissolves companies under 5 levels anyway, it only bites above 5,000 country levels. Together they stand in for "profit below 0.1% of GDP for five years", which script cannot read (no company profit trigger or link): on the 2073 save, of 406 AI companies with five years of history, 102 fell under the profit rule; prosperity alone flags 50 (41 of the 102), both tests 83 (52 of the 102, the rest earning a median 0.34% of GDP). Players are exempt; it models the AI choosing what a player chooses in the companies panel.
- **Flagship subsidies.** The engine's AI almost never changes a subsidy itself. It keeps the ones a building gets by default (ports, railways, power plants) and adds none: on the 2073 save it subsidized 0 of its 23 company buildings (the player 20 of 20), 0 of 140 shipyards, and 0 of 250 airports and highways outside the laws that force them. 12 of the 23 were under half staffed, some losing workers to subsidized buildings nearby, since a building that is not subsidized stops hiring below `BUILDING_DEFAULT_MIN_EARNINGS_TO_HIRE_EMPLOYEES`. `te_company_ai_subsidy_monthly_effect` (`te_company_effects.txt`, same monthly on-action) subsidizes every `bg_company_buildings` building of an AI country that is not in default, and removes those subsidies in default, because the AI never drops one on its own (in default it still paid for 60 of 61 railways). A subsidy only tops a building up to `SUBSIDIES_MIN_GAIN` (0), so a profitable flagship costs nothing. Players are exempt, as for dissolution. The mod's `country_building_*_require_subsidies_bool` forced-subsidy modifiers (airports and highways under Interventionism and Command Economy) held on only 35 of 52 such AI buildings in that save, so this uses `set_subsidized` on each building instead.
- **Verifying in an observer run:** company counts per country and companies lost while the country survived, from saves (the 2026-10-06 study's decoders: company records key `company_type` 0x5c80, prosperity 14193, `prosperous` 24210), and flagship counts with `scripts/analysis/save_world_report.py buildings`. Flagship subsidies: in a plain-text save each building record under `building_manager` carries `subsidized=yes` when subsidized; an AI flagship should have it unless its country is `in_default`.

## Decolonization System (`je_colonial_empire`)

### Overview

The decolonization system models the decline of colonial empires through a journal entry with a stability progress bar, scripted buttons for colonial policies, and a series of events. The system is designed so that **most colonial powers except the top 2-3 GPs will lose most of their colonies** after the `decolonization` tech is researched (era 6).

### Architecture

Three layers:
1. **Gate & Progress Bar:** `je_colonial_empire` activates when a country has colonial states and `decolonization` tech. The `colonial_stability_bar` (0-100, starts at 50) tracks how stable the empire is. At 0 the empire collapses; at 100 it solidifies.
2. **Player Interaction:** the **Colonial Stability widget** (`gui/journal_entry_widgets/colonial_empire_widget.gui`) is the human surface, laid out to `docs/guides/gui_style_guide.md`: an overview at the top (band, programmes running, the stability bar with where it is heading in 12 months at this month's rate, green while rising and red while falling, and a thin line at the next band's edge, in the top band the months held at 100 and the months Solidified against their targets, the condemners' and supporters' shares of great-power prestige), then why stability is moving (each term a pillar bar over its range, then the eligible territories as a collapsed list, largest GDP first, with each one's population, primary-culture share, standard of living and GDP against the colony tests), international pressure, the three programmes (each a level, 0-3, with a minus and a plus), the three decolonization decisions, two history charts and a collapsed How the Colonial Empire Works. The 3 decision buttons behind it carry `is_ai = yes` and are the **AI's** path to the decisions; the AI steps the programmes in a monthly review (§ Colonial Programmes below). Every surface calls the same `colonial_empire_possible_*` / `colonial_empire_effect_*` helpers. Full reference: `journal_entry_systems.md` § Colonial Stability Widget.
   **Timeline Extended window (2026-09-30; not yet seen in game).** The same panels also show on the *Colonial Empire* tab of the mod's own window (`gui/te_systems_window.gui`), under `datacontext = "[GetPlayerJournalEntry('je_colonial_empire')]"`, then a link back to the entry. The tab is greyed until the entry runs; its tooltip lists `ce_entry_unlocked` (the entry's technology and `possible`).
3. **Events:** 21 events fire from `decolonization_events_on_action` (monthly pulse), covering colonial negotiations, crackdowns, releases, GP stance choices, post-independence transitions, and the Suez Crisis model. `decolonization_events.400` / `.401` are the confirmation popups the three decisions open — each previews candidates and offers a "Reconsider" option, so nothing is released by pressing a button.

### Files

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `common/journal_entries/je_colonial_empire.txt` | JE definition, bar, widget mounts, monthly pulse, outcomes |
| `common/scripted_progress_bars/extra_progress_bars.txt` | `colonial_stability_bar`; its `monthly_progress` consumes the 21 leaf script values plus the monthly cap |
| `common/script_values/colonial_empire_values.txt` | Colony counts, programme levels and costs (PROGRAMME LEVELS / PROGRAMME COSTS), the AI review's ceiling and weights, `colonial_stability_term_*` leaves, `colonial_stability_drift_*` groups, the stability-band ladder (`colonial_empire_live_tier` / `_live_next_boundary`), the guarded programme-month counters, display values |
| `common/scripted_triggers/colonial_empire_triggers.txt` | Macro-regions, `is_overseas_colonial_state`, the `colonial_empire_possible_*` gates (3 decisions, an up and a down per programme), the AI review's `colonial_ai_*` checks |
| `common/scripted_buttons/colonial_empire_buttons.txt` | 3 AI-only decision buttons; `possible` / `effect` delegate to the shared helpers. The programmes have no buttons |
| `common/scripted_effects/decolonization.txt` | `form_decolonized_country`, JE cleanup, the decision actions, the programme steps (`colonial_programme_*_up` / `_down` cores, `colonial_empire_effect_*_up` / `_down` for the widget), `colonial_programmes_refresh_modifiers`, `colonial_ai_review_programmes`, the civil-war sync |
| `common/scripted_effects/colonial_empire_display_effects.txt` | `colonial_empire_refresh_display` — the single site deriving all widget display state |
| `common/scripted_guis/colonial_empire_sguis.txt` | 8 handlers backing the widget (2 guards, 3 programme-state reads, 1 text renderer, 2 op-coded actions) |
| `common/customizable_localization/colonial_empire_custom_loc.txt` | Band names, status line and phase-modifier line, keyed on `colonial_empire_live_tier`, the band ladder in `colonial_empire_values.txt`, read live |
| `common/scripted_effects/te_history_colonial_effects.txt` | `te_history_record_colonial_samples` — the two chart series |
| `common/scripted_effects/colonial_collapse_effects.txt` | Colonial collapse: tiny, poor AI countries absorbed or decentralized in the decolonization era |
| `common/laws/colonial_empire_law_injections.txt` | Per-law colonial-stability and programme-effectiveness contributions |
| `common/modifier_type_definitions/colonial_empire_modifier_types.txt` | The four `country_colonial_*` aggregate modifier types |
| `common/on_actions/extra_on_actions.txt` | `decolonization_events_on_action` wiring |
| `events/decolonization_events.txt` | All 21 decolonization events plus the `.400` / `.401` confirmations |
| `events/te_debug_colonial_empire_events.txt` | Console harness: `event te_debug_colonial_empire.1` (bands) / `.2` (pressure, decisions) / `.3` (programme costs, the AI review) |
| `common/static_modifiers/extra_modifiers.txt` | 40+ decolonization modifiers |

### Colonial Programmes (levels, costs, AI)

Three programmes: Development Investment (`colonial_development_investment_modifier`), Military Garrison (`colonial_military_garrison_modifier`) and Cultural Assimilation (`colonial_cultural_assimilation_modifier`). Each runs at a **level from 0 to 3** (`colonial_invest_level` / `colonial_garrison_level` / `colonial_assim_level`; read through the guarded `colonial_*_level_value`). **Level 3 is the full programme**, the on/off programme of before 2026-10, and each level is a third of it, linearly: the bar term (`colonial_stability_term_*` = full-strength effectiveness × level ÷ 3), the static modifier (applied at multiplier 0.333 / 0.667 / 1 by `colonial_programme_apply_modifier`) and the upkeep. Base full-strength terms: Investment +0.5, Garrison +1.0, Assimilation +0.7 a month (laws move them), so all three at the top add 2.2, as before.

**One refresh site.** `colonial_programmes_refresh_modifiers` re-applies the three modifiers at their levels and Investment's expense line, once per effect block: from a player's step (`colonial_programme_after_step`), from the entry's monthly pulse after the AI review, and from the civil-war sync. Each step is a core (`colonial_programme_<p>_up` / `_down`: the payments and the level change) plus, for the widget, the refresh (`colonial_empire_effect_<p>_up` / `_down`); the AI review calls the cores, so no block refreshes twice (re-removing a modifier added earlier in the same block is unproven).

**Costs. `country_expenses_add` is weekly and `gdp` yearly**, so every money value divides a yearly figure by 52. Until 2026-10 Investment charged `gdp × 0.005` a week, commented as monthly: 26% of GDP a year at any size (a play-test AI with GDP £2.314B and two small colonies paid £11.57M a week, over half its spending).

| Cost | Value | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Investment upkeep, per level | 5% a year of the colonies' yearly GDP (Σ `gdp` of our overseas colonial states, cached monthly in `colonial_dev_colonial_gdp`), the base never under 1% of our GDP; ÷ 52 a week | `colonial_invest_level_cost`; the expense line's multiplier is `var:colonial_invest_cost_week` |
| Investment one-off | 26 weeks of one level's upkeep, every step up, on the cost base the row shows (cached monthly and when the entry starts) | `colonial_invest_step_cost` |
| Assimilation one-off | 13 weeks of one Investment level's upkeep, every step up | `colonial_assim_step_cost` |
| Garrison | 100 authority a level (the modifier's 300 × level ÷ 3); a step up needs authority > 100 | `colonial_garrison_authority_per_level` |
| Starting a programme (0 → 1 only) | Investment: Industrialists' and Petite Bourgeoisie's disapproval; Garrison: +5 infamy | the up cores |

As a share of GDP a year per Investment level: 0.5% when the colonies make a tenth of GDP (the target for a typical empire; 1.5% at level 3), 1.25% at a quarter, 0.05% on the floor (colonies under 1%). The play-test case is on the floor: £22.3k a week a level, £66.8k at level 3. A Cultural Hegemony funding level, for scale, is about 0.26%.

**The AI review** (`colonial_ai_review_programmes`, monthly from the pulse, `is_ai = yes`; the six AI-only enable/disable buttons are gone). The ceiling `colonial_ai_programme_ceiling` is the monthly cap (1.667) while the bar is below 100 and `colonial_ai_full_bar_margin` (+0.5, the war term) at 100: at a full bar a level only matters while it keeps the change safely positive, because a dip below 100 restarts the 60-month completion count, and a change trimmed to 0 would dip at the first war. The change it compares is the widget's `colonial_stability_drift_uncapped_display`.
1. **Lower** (Investment, Assimilation, Garrison, one level each at most, re-reading the change between them): when the change without the top level still reaches the ceiling (`colonial_ai_drift_without_<p>_step`), a level adds nothing (laws), the colonies are gone, or the programme is unaffordable (Investment: in default or `net_fixed_income < 0`; Garrison: authority < 0; Assimilation: authority or bureaucracy < 0). A cut for cost holds that programme off for 12 months (`colonial_ai_hold_<p>`).
2. **Raise** (only if nothing was lowered): while the change is under the ceiling, one programme up a level, by weight among those it can carry (`colonial_ai_can_raise_<p>`: the up gate, a positive term, out of default and `net_fixed_income` above one level's upkeep for Investment, authority > 34 and a bureaucracy surplus above one level's cut (5% of produced bureaucracy) for Assimilation). The weights are the old buttons' `ai_chance` (`colonial_ai_<p>_raise_weight`), read with `value =` in the `random_list` (vanilla's form; `add = <named script value>` there logs "Malformed token").
The raise and lower rules leave a dead band one level wide, so a programme does not flip back and forth.

**Endings.** The programme-month counters (`colonial_invest_months` etc.) add level ÷ 3 a month, so a month at level 3 counts 1 as before and a cheap low level cannot steer the ending as fast.

**Old saves.** A save holding a programme modifier but no level gets level 3 (`decol_migrate_programmes`, top of the pulse) and pays the new upkeep. Until that pulse, `colonial_<p>_level_value` reads the modifier as level 3 and each step core starts a missing level from it, so a click in the first month neither zeroes the programme nor strips the other two. Leftover `<modifier>_held` records are inert.

### Stability Bar Formula (`colonial_stability_bar.monthly_progress`)

**This table is the formula.** Every term is a named leaf script value in `common/script_values/colonial_empire_values.txt` (country scope), and the bar's `monthly_progress` is nothing but 21 unconditional `add = { desc = "<tooltip key>" value = owner.<leaf> }` lines, plus a 22nd, last line for the monthly cap (below). Each leaf returns 0 when its gate is false. The widget and the history charts read the **same** leaves through the nine `colonial_stability_drift_*` group sums, so there is exactly one place each number lives.

To retune a term, change only its leaf. To add one: add a leaf, add it to its group, add one `add` line to the bar with a `desc` key, and add a row here.

| Leaf (`colonial_stability_term_…`) | Group | Gate | Value | Tooltip key |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_base` | base | — | **-0.5** | `colonial_base_drift_tt` |
| `_laws` | laws | — | `modifier:country_colonial_stability_drift_add` | `colonial_law_aggregate_tt` |
| `_ig_landowners` | igs | Landowners powerful | **+0.3** | `colonial_ig_landowners_strong_tt` |
| `_ig_armed_forces` | igs | Armed Forces powerful | **+0.2** | `colonial_ig_armed_forces_strong_tt` |
| `_ig_intelligentsia` | igs | Intelligentsia powerful | **-0.4** | `colonial_ig_intelligentsia_strong_tt` |
| `_ig_unions` | igs | Trade Unions powerful | **-0.3** | `colonial_ig_unions_strong_tt` |
| `_overreach` | overreach | `colonial_overreach_ratio > 0` | ratio × **-0.4** | `colonial_overreach_tt` |
| `_gp_rank` | rank | great power | **+0.3** | `colonial_gp_rank_bonus_tt` |
| `_non_gp` | rank | not a great power | **-0.5** | `colonial_non_gp_penalty_tt` |
| `_gp_condemnation` | gp | — (0 with no condemners) | `colonial_gp_condemnation_weight` × **-0.6** | `colonial_gp_condemnation_tt` |
| `_gp_high_pressure` | gp | condemners' prestige share ≥ 1/3 | **-1.0** | `colonial_gp_high_pressure_tt` |
| `_gp_extreme_pressure` | gp | condemners' prestige share ≥ 2/3 | **-2.0** | `colonial_gp_extreme_pressure_tt` |
| `_gp_support` | gp | — (0 with no supporters) | `colonial_gp_support_weight` × **+0.3** | `colonial_gp_support_tt` |
| `_garrison` | policies | Garrison level ≥ 1 | `modifier:country_colonial_garrison_effectiveness_add` × level ÷ 3 | `colonial_garrison_aggregate_tt` |
| `_assim` | policies | Assimilation level ≥ 1 | `modifier:country_colonial_assim_effectiveness_add` × level ÷ 3 | `colonial_assim_aggregate_tt` |
| `_invest` | policies | Investment level ≥ 1 | `modifier:country_colonial_invest_effectiveness_add` × level ÷ 3 | `colonial_invest_aggregate_tt` |
| `_low_acceptance` | acceptance | low-acceptance colonies > 0 | count × **-0.4** | `colonial_low_acceptance_tt` |
| `_high_acceptance` | acceptance | high-acceptance colonies > 0 | count × **+0.5** | `colonial_high_acceptance_tt` |
| `_war` | domestic | at war | **-0.5** | `colonial_war_penalty_tt` |
| `_revolution` | domestic | revolution | **-1.0** | `colonial_revolution_penalty_tt` |
| `_turmoil` | domestic | `country_turmoil > 0.05` | turmoil × **-2.0** | `colonial_turmoil_penalty_tt` |
| `_cap` (**last line**) | — (not in a group) | sum outside ±`colonial_stability_drift_cap` | clamp(sum, ±1.667) − sum | `colonial_drift_cap_tt` |

**Great-power terms scale with relative prestige.** Each condemning or supporting great power is weighted by its prestige divided by the empire's (floored at 1), clamped to **[0.25, 2.0]** — `colonial_gp_condemnation_weight` / `colonial_gp_support_weight` sum those weights. A peer great power therefore contributes the table's figure (-0.6 / +0.3), one with double the empire's prestige or more contributes twice that, and one with a quarter or less contributes a quarter. Net effect: the most prestigious empires shrug off condemnation from lesser powers, while small colonial holders (Portugal, Belgium, the Netherlands) feel superpower condemnation at up to -1.2/month each.

**The two escalations fire on prestige share, not head count.** `colonial_gp_condemner_prestige_share` is the condemners' combined prestige divided by `colonial_gp_prestige_pool` — the prestige of every great power *plus the empire itself*, counted even when it is not a great power. At **≥ 1/3** `_gp_high_pressure` applies a flat -1.0; at **≥ 2/3** `_gp_extreme_pressure` adds a further -2.0 (both apply, -3.0 total). Because the empire sits in the pool, a dominant empire is hard to isolate, while a minor one reaches the thresholds as soon as a few big powers condemn it. The pool is great powers rather than world prestige on purpose: condemners are a subset of the great powers that excludes the empire, and great powers together hold well under all of world prestige, so a two-thirds world share would practically never fire. `colonial_empire_refresh_display` snapshots the share into `colonial_empire_condemner_share` for the widget's overview pie and International Pressure section, and the supporters' share of the same pool (`colonial_gp_supporter_prestige_share`, display only: no escalation reads it) into `colonial_empire_supporter_share`, the pie's second slice. The event and button AI weights keyed on `colonial_gp_condemners_count >= N` are unchanged — they count powers, not pressure.

**Monthly cap, applied last.** However large the sum of the 21 terms, the bar moves at most **±`colonial_stability_drift_cap` = 1.667** a month (100 ÷ 60), so crossing the whole bar takes at least five years either way. The cap acts on the *total*, after everything is summed — not on any one term — so a sudden spike (a superpower condemning a tiny colony) cannot empty the bar in months, but a big enough sum still outweighs the empire's programmes and it has to change the inputs (win the great power over, integrate or release colonies) to turn the trend. The bar applies it as the `_cap` row: `colonial_stability_drift_total` (capped) minus `colonial_stability_drift_uncapped`, which is 0 inside the cap, so the hover shows how much the cap absorbed. `colonial_stability_drift_total` is now the capped figure everywhere it is read (the `colonial_empire_d_total` snapshot and the signed history chart, rescaled to ±2); the widget's headline is `colonial_stability_drift_total_display` and its breakdown ends with `colonial_stability_drift_cap_display`. The cap leaf evaluates the uncapped sum twice, i.e. every leaf about three times a month — acceptable at monthly cadence.

**Contributions that are not rows above** reach the bar through `country_colonial_stability_drift_add` (the `_laws` leaf) and surface inside that line's own `GetValueWithBreakdownFor` breakdown: every contributing law (Colonial Affairs, Minority Rights, Citizenship, Distribution of Power, Free Speech, Internal Security), the era techs (`globalization` **-1.5**/mo, `knowledge_economy` **-0.75**/mo), and the timed `colonial_stability_positive_event` / `_negative_event` modifiers. Programme effectiveness likewise aggregates a `base_values` baseline plus per-law contributions: the programme's full-strength figure, of which each level runs a third (§ Colonial Programmes).

**Reading it in game:** hovering the bar shows all 21 terms and the cap with their current values — `GetPeriodicProgressBreakdown`, generated by the engine from the `desc` keys above. The widget shows the nine group sums, the cap row and the projected (capped) total.

**Design intent:** A typical GP with 5 colonies nets roughly -0.5 to -1.0/month even with programmes active. Only great powers with few, well-integrated colonies and little GP condemnation can stabilize. The era techs make holding colonies nearly impossible late.

### Events (1-21)

Events 1-15 handle the core colonial cycle: negotiations, crackdowns, releases, GP stances, cultural identity, post-independence economics, nationalization.

**Post-Independence Events (16-21):**
- **Event 16 "The Partition Question"** — New nations near neighbors with shared heritage can demand unification (claims+tension), propose federation (truces+goodwill), or accept borders (stability).
- **Event 17 "Whose Country Is This?"** — Border disputes between two recently-formed nations. Options: press claims, seek mediation, accept borders.
- **Event 18 "The Strongman's Promise"** — Political instability in new nations. Options: military coup (authority+SoL loss), democratic transition (legitimacy), one-party state (authority+research).
- **Event 19 "The Crisis" (Suez model)** — GP reacts when a former colony nationalizes assets. Options: military intervention (infamy 15, **triggers Event 20** for all other GPs), economic sanctions (infamy 5), accept outcome. This is the flagship "interactive" GP event.
- **Event 20 "Gunboats in the Harbor"** — Other GPs respond to military intervention. Options: condemn (+moral authority, relations penalties to intervener), support intervention, stay neutral. **Not in on_actions** — triggered directly by Event 19 option A.
- **Event 21 "The Non-Aligned Path"** — New nations choose between competing superpowers or non-alignment.

**Choices made by the other party (2026-09, event-agency work, #427):**
- **Former colonies choose first.** The overlord's pool slots that used to fire `.5` (closer ties) and `.6` (reparations) now fire precursors on the former colony: **`.60`** (propose ties) and **`.61`** (demand reparations). The overlord hears only if the colony asked.
  - Lockout variables on the overlord replace the old cooldowns: `decol_ties_overture_cd` and `decol_reparations_demand_cd`.
  - Reparations are a real transfer. The claim is fixed at demand time: 10 % of the payer's yearly revenue, capped at 10 % of the claimant's GDP (`decol_reparations_value`).
- **Truces need consent.** `.16.b` (federation) and `.17.c` (border) ask the neighbour first, through **`.62`** / **`.63`**, instead of imposing a 60-month truce on it.
- **Pressure comes from real stances.** `.2`'s pressuring power is picked only from great powers carrying `gp_anti_colonial_stance`. The General Assembly flavour appears only once the decolonization UN regime exists.
- **`.20` names the real intervener and victim,** saved by `.19.A`, instead of re-picking them.
- **Only colonial subjects count.** `.1`/`.2`/`.3`/`.8`/`.11` iterate `is_qualifying_colonial_subject` only.
- **`.206`'s "by decision" epilogue** needs a chosen release. `decol_record_chosen_release` counts them (`decol_chosen_releases`); otherwise a neutral variant shows.

**What a revolution's winner keeps (PR #508):** the three running programmes, the permanent ending rewards (`colonial_empire_solidified_modifier` and the three path modifiers), `imperial_federation_modifier` and `mandate_system_modifier` are country modifiers, which a civil war's winner does not inherit. Each has a variable record, and `decol_repair_after_civil_war` (from `te_civil_war_on_won`) puts back each modifier whose record the winner holds and whose modifier it lacks. The permanent modifiers' records are `<modifier>_held`, written by `decol_grant_permanent_modifier`, plus the decisions' own `imperial_federation_taken` / `mandate_system_taken`. A programme's record is its level variable (`colonial_invest_level`, `colonial_garrison_level`, `colonial_assim_level`), and `decol_sync_programmes` rebuilds the modifiers from the levels. The programmes need the entry, and whether `on_civil_war_won` runs before or after the winner inherits it is unknown, so the entry's monthly pulse refreshes them too; its programme tests read the levels, so a programme it restores counts that month. The permanent modifiers' records are country variables, merged before `on_civil_war_won`, so the repair restores them at once. A yearly country pulse (`decol_permanent_modifier_sync_on_action`) backfills the records on saves from before they existed. It backfills the Solidified record only while no entry runs, because that key is also the entry's top band modifier. Timed modifiers and the entry's band modifiers are not rebuilt.

**Former colonies, colonial wars and notices (2026-09, #430):**
- **Every freed colony is a former colony.** `decol_mark_former_colony` (`decolonization.txt`) sets `var:former_overlord` (untimed) and `recently_decolonized` (7300 days), the shapes the vanilla release actions set in `te_construction_market_on_released_*`. `apply_decolonization_path` calls it for every country `form_decolonized_country` creates, and so do the `make_independent` options `.1.c`, `.1.d_neo`, `.3.a` and `.3.c`. That opens the precursors `.60` / `.61` (and, through them, `.5` / `.6`) and the post-independence events `.7`, `.16`, `.17`, `.18` and `.21`, which read `recently_decolonized`.
- **Events for countries that never hold the entry gate on the rule.** `.4`, `.7`, `.15`, `.16`, `.17`, `.18`, `.21` (a former colony) and `.14`, `.20` (any great power) test `has_game_rule = decolonization_enabled` instead of `has_journal_entry = je_colonial_empire`. #44 had gated every event in the file on the entry, so until 2026-09 these could not fire: a freed colony never holds the entry, and `.14`'s AI weights and `.20`'s audience are aimed at great powers without colonies. `.14` / `.20` also reach a great power whose own entry has ended, on purpose: they stand for the powers that disapprove of colonial empires and press them (owner ruling, 2026-09-26). `.19` (the former overlord) keeps the entry gate, as do the metropole-side events.
- **The former-colony events need only the world's decolonization era.** `.4`, `.7`, `.15`, `.16`–`.19`, `.21` and the outer gate of `decolonization_events_on_action` test `decol_era_begun` (some country has researched `decolonization`, `colonial_empire_triggers.txt`) instead of the recipient's own research (owner ruling, 2026-09-26). The metropole-side events still need the recipient's own technology through their own triggers (the entry's `is_shown_when_inactive`, `.14`, the `.60` / `.61` target checks).
- **`.51` (Conscription Crisis) fires only in a colonial war.** The trigger is `decol_fighting_colonial_war` (`colonial_empire_triggers.txt`), used by both the event and its pool entry. It is true when the country is at war and one of these holds: the colonial crackdown is running, an enemy is its own qualifying colonial subject, or an enemy is a secessionist people sharing no heritage with it. Other wars still drain the bar through `colonial_stability_term_war`.
- **`.50`'s City-and-sterling text is Britain's** (`c:GBR ?= this`). Every other empire gets a generic treasury text, since the mod models no per-country currency.
- **Notices name the colonial power.** `.1`, `.2`, `.3` and `.11` save it as `scope:decol_colonial_power` for their `notification_*` loc. A message reads only `notification_<msg>_name/_desc/_tooltip`, never a bare key. `.2.c` frees no one, so it posts its own `colonial_neocolonial_reforms_notice`; `colonial_neocolonial_terms_notice` is for the options that grant independence (`.1.d_neo`, `.3.c`).
- **The tech drops our claims on decentralized states.** `decolonization` carries `country_remove_decentralized_claims_bool` (a marker with no engine effect, defined in `mod_entity_modifier_types.txt`). `tech_marker_grants_on_action` (monthly, `extra_on_actions.txt`) reads it: a country that has the marker and any claim on a decentralized country's state removes each such claim (`state_region = { remove_claim = ROOT }`) and gets the `decentralized_claims_renounced` notice. It is a standing rule, not a one-shot: a claim that arrives after the first sweep goes within the month. The stake action itself is closed to a country with the marker: `te_stake_colonial_claim.txt` `REPLACE`s vanilla `da_stake_colonial_claim` with a `custom_tooltip` requirement (`da_stake_colonial_claim_renounced_tt`) added to its `possible`, so neither the player nor the AI pays for a claim the sweep would remove (the action's `accept_effect` also creates an incident against the target). It is a REPLACE, copied from the installed 1.14.5 game, because an INJECT of a second `possible` block is rejected at load (`scripting_best_practices.md` § "A second trigger block is rejected at load"); re-diff it on every vanilla patch. The sweep is the backstop for claims that arrive another way. Claims on regions with only recognized or unrecognized owners are untouched. A split region with any decentralized portion (such as Equateur shared with Sudan) loses the entire region claim, because claims belong to state regions. There is no claim iterator, so the sweep uses `any_decentralized_country` / `every_decentralized_country` and walks those countries' states. Both the pre-check and removal filter enter `state_region`, then ask the claimer via `ROOT = { has_claim = PREV }`; `PREV` there is the region, not a state's individual owner. `number_of_claims > 0` and the pre-check keep countries with nothing to drop off the removal pass.

### AI Behavior Tuning

AI weights across events are tuned to favor decolonization:
- Release/negotiate options have base weights of 2-4 with bonuses for non-GPs and high GP condemnation
- Crackdown/suppress options have base weights of 1
- The `ce_planned_decolonization` button fires when 3+ GPs condemn or 3+ states have low acceptance; non-GPs get an extra +30 weight bonus
- GP stance event (14): condemn colonialism base 5 with knowledge_economy bonus; support base 0

## Independence Nationalization Event (`decolonization_events.15`)

- **Purpose:** When a country gains independence, presents a choice about foreign-owned assets.
- **Trigger:** `on_become_independent` on_action (any subject that becomes independent), and `apply_decolonization_path` a week after it forms a country (release decisions and collapses; the new country was never a subject, so `on_become_independent` may not reach it).
- **Condition:** the decolonization game rule, the world's decolonization era (`decol_era_begun`), not a subject, no nationalization in the last 20 years (`nationalization_cooldown_var`, which also stops a double firing), and at least one foreign minor power or above owns >5% of the new country's GDP.
- **Branching description:** Uses `first_valid` + `triggered_desc` — violent version if `is_at_war = yes`, peaceful version otherwise.
- **Options:**
  - **(A) Full nationalization:** Seize all foreign assets. +10 infamy, -60 relations with all foreign owners, prestige boost, throughput penalty. Loyalists among lower strata, trade unions approve, industrialists oppose.
  - **(B) Selective nationalization:** Strategic sectors only. +5 infamy, -30 relations, moderate prestige boost.
  - **(C) Protect foreign property:** No infamy, +20 relations, foreign investment continues. Radicals among lower strata, industrialists approve.
- **Modifiers:** `independence_nationalization_modifier`, `selective_nationalization_modifier`, `foreign_property_protected_modifier` (all timed, decaying).
- **Notification:** `nationalization_notice` sent to affected foreign countries.
- **Files:** `events/decolonization_events.txt` (event 15), `common/on_actions/extra_on_actions.txt` (hook), `common/static_modifiers/extra_modifiers.txt` (3 modifiers), `common/messages/extra_messages.txt` (notification).

## Dynamic Treaty Names
